# Supplementary Material

> **This document is the Supplementary Material of the manuscript**, submitted together with it.
> **Numbering convention**: the main text uses §n for sections and S n for subsections of this document.

## 0. Generation and verifiability (**read this section first**)

This document **was not written by hand**: the body text of every section **embeds verbatim** the content of the following **verified source files**,
so "the numbers in this document" and "the numbers in the source files" **cannot be inconsistent**. Every source file records an md5 and a byte count, so anyone can re-check it.

| Subsection | Content | Source file | md5 | Bytes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **S1** | S1 · Per-row marking table and mechanical recomputation over 19 public benchmarks × 4 counting units | `split_units_19rows_20260916.md` | `2d542d10226347dfffb69dbab8b61d1a` | 5805 |
| **S2** | S2 · Correction record for the counting convention (old convention → recomputed convention) | `the audit-correction and adjudication record, §2 superseding block` | `94e85a41a6f7c5a5b6788a11b96c2c8e` | 24638 |
| **S3** | S3 · Per-row evidence from the cross-framework configuration audit (5 frameworks / 25 official files) | `xframe_evidence_20260916.txt` | `78b3e251f5d4c8db3b6378ecbbd2b3e0` | 13361 |
| **S4** | S4 · 2×2 evaluation matrix over the three-split protocol (41 runs × {best,last} × {val,test}) | `xeval_analysis_20260916.txt` | `8b74b2eb6228c078d5a38131d119567b` | 8534 |
| **S5** | S5 · Selection premium on public third-party logs (sample and how to read it) | `selection_premium_public_20260916.md` | `04950c92d1ab4bcfb5bbe616b2dcbec4` | 9479 |

**Generation script**: `work\gen_supplementary_20260916.py` (purely local and re-runnable; S5 reads the collaborating archive in read-only mode).
**Re-check entry point**: see §S7 of this document.

### Terms this paper uses (one place, so they need not be reconstructed)

| term | what it means here |
|---|---|
| **arm** | one member of a paired comparison. The **baseline** arm follows the published convention; the **strategy** arm is the intervention under test (e.g. a learning-rate change). "Baseline" does **not** mean unmodified software — both arms run the same code |
| **cell** | one (corpus pair × arm) unit of the design. A cell name such as `shwd2sf` reads **source→target** (SHWD → SFCHD); the corpora are `dota15`, `aitod`, `visdrone`, `smoke`, `shwd`, `sfchd`, `mende`, `mask` |
| **`best` / `last`** | the two checkpoints compared throughout: `best.pt`, written by the training script by **fitness** on the monitored split, and the final epoch |
| **selection premium** | `best − last` **within a run**: what selecting the best checkpoint adds, measured on the split the numbers are reported from |
| **Δgap** | the paired **difference of the premium between the two arms**, per corpus. Positive means `val` **understates** the strategy arm's effect (§5.1, Tier 2) |
| **realization rate** | `prem_test / prem_val`, aggregated over cells (§8.6); the share of the premium selected on `val` that survives on a disjoint `test` |
| **κ** | through-origin slope of `ΔT` on `ΔV` over a run's epochs (§8.7): how much of a run's movement on `val` appears on `test` |
| **same directory / alias** | a dataset config whose `val:` and `test:` point at the same path, so the checkpoint is selected on the split it is scored on |
| **`shapeiou`** | the shape-aware IoU variant used in the registered replication's strategy arm (§9) |
| **`sio_b_results.csv`** | the per-run results table of the clean-protocol batch; its unwritten endpoint convention is disclosed as a defect in §10 |
| **FROZEN-HASH** | the registration's self-referential stamp: the md5 of the registration text above the stamp line (§9, verifiable by re-hashing that text) |

## S1 · Per-row marking table and mechanical recomputation over 19 public benchmarks × 4 counting units

> **Purpose**: main text §3, §6; this is the **complete per-row evidence** for the claim "how many benchmarks are problematic".
> **Source**: `split_units_19rows_20260916.md` (md5 `2d542d10226347dfffb69dbab8b61d1a`, 5805 bytes); the content below is **embedded verbatim, unmodified**.

```text
# 19-row × 5-column unit marking table (front matter of the companion paper)

> Generated by `work/recompute_split_units_20260916.py`; all percentages are mechanically recomputed from the markings in this table, with nothing hard-coded.
> Established 2026-09-16. The (h-1) ruling has been applied (D-Fire counts as clean).

## The three units (the main text must state which one it uses)

| Unit | Definition |
|---|---|
| **release** | The benchmark's own release layer: whether the official split artifacts/scripts provide an independent held-out test |
| **protocol** | The official protocol layer: the split declared by the official source, and whether that test can be self-evaluated locally |
| **yolo_dist** | Generic YOLO distribution-package layer (= the column measured in the §1 table of the audit, i.e. column 4) |
| **reported** | The actually-reported layer: which split the numbers reported in the literature come from, and whether they are independent of checkpoint selection |

Evidence marks: (A) explicit in the audit｜(D) derived from audit facts｜(?) the audit takes no position

## Marking table

| # | benchmark | release | protocol | yolo_dist | reported | Evidence category | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

     17 WIDER FACE [47], 18 CrowdHuman [48], 19 D-Fire [49] -->

| 2 | PASCAL VOC | `n_a(A)` | `test_gated(A)` | `alias(A)` | `alias(A)` | mirror VOC.yaml (both keys cited per row) | high |
| 3 | Objects365 | `no_test(A)` | `no_test(A)` | `no_test_key(A)` | `alias(D)` | mirror yaml + Ultralytics docs | high |
| 4 | Open Images v7 | `independent_test(A)` | `independent_test(A)` | `no_test_key(A)` | `alias(D)` | Official facts page + mirror yaml | high |

| 8 | AI-TOD | `contradictory(A)` | `contradictory(A)` | `unknown(A)` | `unknown(?)` | Official README (contains two mutually contradictory release statements) | low |
| 9 | UAVDT | `no_val(A)` | `no_val(A)` | `unknown(A)` | `alias(A)` | Official paper + two mirrors (the audit calls these unverified beyond these two) | medium |
| 10 | xView | `no_test(A)` | `no_test(A)` | `no_test_key(A)` | `alias(A)` | mirror xView.yaml | low |
| 11 | DIOR | `independent_test(A)` | `independent_test(A)` | `no_test_key(A)` | `unknown(?)` | Official description (no counting) + mirror DIOR-R yaml | low |
| 12 | NWPU VHR-10 | `no_split(A)` | `no_split(A)` | `unknown(A)` | `alias(A)` | Official TorchGeo (the split means positive/negative) | high |

## Recomputation results

| Unit | alias/non-independent | Among decidable rows | unknown |
|---|---|---|---|
| release (the benchmark's own release layer) | **10/19 = 52.6%** | 10/19 = 52.6% | 0 |
| protocol (official protocol layer) | **13/19 = 68.4%** | 13/19 = 68.4% | 0 |
| yolo (generic YOLO distribution-package layer (yolo_dist)) | **4/19 = 21.1%** | 4/15 = 26.7% | 4 |
| reported (actually-reported layer) | **12/19 = 63.2%** | 12/15 = 80.0% | 4 |

## Cross-check against the numbers already published in the audit

- Audit 3a "literal `val == test` **2/19**" → this table's "the two keys of the distribution-package config point to the same path" (A evidence source) yields **2/19**, **reproduced ✓**
  - The other 1 alias row is produced by a **generator** (Mendeley face-mask), not by a distribution-package config, and is not counted under the 3a convention
- Audit "**14/19**" → this table's `reported` yields **12/19** (difference -2)
- Audit "**5/19** clean" → this table's `reported=clean` yields **3/19** (difference -2)
- The audit's "12/19" was obtained by manually adjusting two rows and is **not** a recomputation under a consistent unit; this table recomputes by a consistent rule, so the values need not be identical

## A self-consistent set of numbers for the main text to adopt

- Literal `val == test` (generic distribution-package config, A evidence source): **2/19 = 11%** — PASCAL VOC, SFCHD
- train/val from the same source, produced by the generator: **1/19** (Mendeley face-mask), **a different convention, which must be stated separately**
- Non-independent at the official protocol layer: **13/19 = 68%**
- Non-independent at the actually-reported layer: **12/19 = 63%** (4 unknown rows) — **13/19** if a
  paper's own `val` numbers, rather than community `test-dev` numbers, are what the unit judges
  for COCO (the main text states both readings)
- Clean at the actually-reported layer: **3/19 = 16%**

## The four levels of "same directory" (resolving (g0))

- **L1** literal `val==test` in a generic distribution package: 2 rows — PASCAL VOC, SFCHD
- **L2** distribution package with `train==val` (F-C): 1 row — SHWD
- **L3** distribution package clean but reported layer non-independent: 9 rows — Objects365, Open Images v7, DOTA v2.0, UAVDT, xView, NWPU VHR-10, MAFA, WIDER FACE, CrowdHuman
- **L4** all the rest: 7 rows

## Corrections already applied

- **(h-1) D-Fire / FireSmoke family**: the "mixed" criterion for the family row is taken from a config of SHWD lineage (A:59 cites Reflective_vests.yaml; A:52 notes that it is based on SHWD). All three copies belonging to FireSmoke/D-Fire are distinct. Under the "release layer" unit explicitly adopted in 3a, D-Fire goes into the clean group.
- **(d-1) AI-TOD / DOTA asymmetry**: the audit marks F-C for AI-TOD, which contains trainval, but does not mark DOTA v1/v2, which likewise contain trainval. This table uniformly adopts the "release/protocol layer" unit: trainval is a user-protocol caveat and is not used as grounds for a verdict; neither place is re-judged, but AI-TOD is contradictory because its v2 release statement contradicts itself.
```

## S2 · Correction record for the counting conventions (old convention → recomputed convention)

> **Purpose**: main text §6.3; explains why the same fact once had two sets of numbers, and which set has been invalidated.
> **Source**: `the audit-correction and adjudication record, §2 superseding block` (md5 `94e85a41a6f7c5a5b6788a11b96c2c8e`, 24638 bytes); the following content is **embedded verbatim, unmodified**.

```text
> ### The four counts in this section (§2) have been superseded by a mechanical recomputation (note added 2026-09-16)
>
> The **12/19 (release/protocol layer)**, **13/19 (actual reporting layer)**, **6/19 (clean)** and **≤14/19 (upper bound)** in the §2 table
> are inconsistent with the mechanical recomputation completed 4 minutes later, and **must no longer be cited**. The recomputation is the sole counting source: `analysis/eval_validity/split_units_recompute_20260916.txt` and
> `split_units_19rows_20260916.md`
>
> | Unit | Non-independent | Clean | Undecidable |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | Literal `val == test` (the benchmark's own release) | **2/19 ≈ 11%** | — | — |
> | release (release layer) | **10/19 ≈ 53%** | — | — |
> | protocol (official protocol layer) | **13/19 ≈ 68%** | — | — |
> | yolo_dist (generic YOLO distribution package layer) | **4/19 ≈ 21%** | — | 4 |
> | **reported (actual reporting layer)** | **12/19 ≈ 63%** (12/15 = **80%** of the decidable rows) | **3/19 ≈ 16%** | **4** |
>
> The four "undecidable" rows are AI-TOD, DIOR, Mendeley and D-Fire — **the audit expressed no position on their reporting practice**; it does not mean they are clean.
>
> The four concrete discrepancies and their dispositions:
> 1. §2's "release/protocol layer 12/19" does not hold under the recomputed convention — the recomputed release is 10/19 and protocol is 13/19,
>    **12 is neither the one nor the other** (consistent with the ruling in §1(g): it is the result of two rows being hand-adjusted).
> 2. §2's "actual reporting layer 13/19" should be **12/19** (4 rows undecidable).
> 3. §2's "clean 6/19" should be **3/19**; the reason the clean group dropped from 5 to 3 is that those 4 rows **were never addressed by the audit** — this is a finding, not a gap.
> 4. §2's "≤14/19 upper bound" is replaced by a derivable statement: **80% of the decidable rows are non-independent (12/15)**, which is both stronger and more defensible than an unenumerated upper bound.
>
> **"79%" has already been ruled inadmissible in §1(e)**; this is confirmed here as well: the main text uses only the four enumerated points in the table above.

> **⚠️ This section was revised on 2026-09-16 (second version)**. **The first version (09:56) was written before the 19×5 marking table was built and gave predicted values**
> (release/protocol layer 12/19, actual reporting layer 13/19, clean 6/19). The mechanical recomputation 4 minutes later
> (`eval_validity\split_units_recompute_20260916.txt`, 10:00, with a per-unit 19-row subtotal check) gave different results.
> **The two sets of numbers cannot be cited side by side**. Following the disposition recommendation in §7 #0 of `the asset inventory`,
> **the recomputation is taken as the sole counting source**; the first version's predicted values are retained in the "trail" table below for traceability.
```

```text
========================================================================================================
```

```text
shwd2sf     strat  10     0.865      0.151      18%   +0.69 +0.09 +0.31 +0.20 +0.11 +0.03 -0.09 -0.23 -0.30 +0.71
smoke2sf    base   10     0.691      0.507      73%   +0.44 +0.59 +0.91 +0.20 +0.64 +0.66 +0.96 -0.12 +0.19 +0.60
smoke2sf    strat  10     0.605     -0.158     -26%   +0.00 +0.36 -0.42 -0.33 +0.43 -0.16 -0.34 -0.22 -0.85 -0.04

  Total (**headline convention = run-weighted**, n = 50, all five cells at n = 10): prem_val mean +0.879 pp, prem_test mean +0.205 pp, realization rate **23.3 %**
    (run-level bootstrap 95% CI [8.4 %, 37.4 %], P(rate ≤ 0) = 0.002, so the interval excludes no-realization; script `work/item2_final_v2_20260925.py`)
  The three weightings are **identical** once every cell carries n = 10 (run-weighted ≡ arm-cell-equal ≡ n ≥ 5 only), all three giving **23.3 %**;
    on the previous 41-run sample (aitod20 at n = 1) they gave 19.6 % / 32.4 % / 34.5 %, and the ~15 pp spread came from that n = 1 cell
    — it carried 20 % of the arm-cell weight while contributing a single run to the run-level mean. **Completing that cell removes the spread**, not a change of weighting.
    The cell-level bootstrap interval of the arm-cell-equal estimator is [−17.3 %, 53.2 %] and **includes zero** (only five clusters), while the run-level interval [8.4 %, 37.4 %] does not;
    so "partial realization" is significant only under run weighting (article §8.6).
  Note: this version unifies the total's prem_val on the **declared** convention `V_max − V_final`; the previous version's +0.896 used
    best.pt − last.pt, which gives +0.905 on the same 41 runs (a 0.03 pp difference).
  The previous version's **arm-cell-equal** totals (prem_val +0.883, prem_test +0.172, also over the 41 runs) are superseded by the 23.3 % above:
    with the aitod20 cell completed, arm-cell-equal and run-weighted **are the same estimator**, so it is no longer listed as a separate "sensitivity".
  Cells with **negative** prem_test: 2/5 —— aitod20/base(-0.218), smoke2sf/strat(-0.157)
  ⇒ The gain selected on val is only partly realized on an independent split, and can even reverse.
```

```text
**Logs not included in the statistics** (too few epochs to speak of "selection"): `aakashchalla12345678-yolov8n__unstated__yolov8n.csv` (30 epochs), `dhanduramesh-yolov11s__ppe-css-data__yolo11n.csv` (1 epoch), `kimmkless-yolodetector__train4-face-expression__yolov8n.csv` (5 epochs), `ottogalt-yolo-result__unstated__yolov8.csv` (20 epochs)

## Reading

- Complete logs (≥40 epochs) number **20** in total, coming from **6** source repositories; the median premium is **+1.04 pp**, the maximum **+5.65 pp**, the minimum **+0.00 pp**; **18/20** are positive.
- Distribution: +0.00, +0.00, +0.10, +0.14, +0.16, +0.31, +0.57, +0.70, +0.74, +0.91, +1.18, +1.33, +1.42, +1.54, +1.55, +2.07, +2.16, +2.36, +2.84, +5.65
- **The most important point**: the largest single premium **+5.65 pp** appears in an ordinary public release log, whereas the effects reported in this kind of literature are often only of the order of 0.5–2 pp——that is, **the selection component can be of the same order as, or even larger than, the reported effect**. The median +1.04 pp is already not small. This explains why "on which split the reported value was selected" is not a detail.
- But the sample is still small (20 logs, 6 sources, and most sources do not annotate the dataset/protocol), so it **can only** be written as "this component is present and quantifiable in other people's published logs"; it **cannot** provide a field-level distribution, nor **can** benchmark aliasing be inferred from it.

## Verifiability

- **Byte-level verifiable**: the **SHA-256 and byte count** of every log are given in `SHA256SUMS.txt` in the same directory (retrieval date 2026-09-16).
  Anyone can check whether "the bytes analysed" and "the bytes in circulation" agree.
- **Provenance completed**: `PROVENANCE.md` in the same directory has now been written. **All** 24 files are matched to **specific source paths** by content hash or unique byte count (8 Kaggle datasets + 1 GitHub repo, pinned at commit `2417aff0457c`), with 0 untraced entries. The mapping is not guessed from filename similarity: the script hashes **every entry** in `_work\kg\*.zip` and collides it against the sha256 of the local files. Check: `python work/build_provenance.py`.
- Two things that **still** cannot be derived from this directory (see `PROVENANCE.md` §4): ① the **exact downloaded version** on the Kaggle side;
  ② whether the `val`/`test` of these benchmarks are in the same directory (the logs do not carry a yaml).

## What is still missing (to be stated as well when writing the main text)

1. **Sample size**: 20 logs, 6 sources——enough to show that the component exists in other people's logs and to give its order of magnitude, but not enough to give a field-level distribution; discussing a distribution requires several tens of traceable logs.
2. **Protocol unknown**: public logs carry no yaml, so it is impossible to judge whether the `val` and `test` of a run are the same directory;
   therefore "the premium exists" and "benchmark aliasing" are two independent propositions and cannot stand in for each other.
3. **Endpoint convention**: the logs give only per-epoch validation values; public repositories usually do not record "which epoch was reported",
   so the premium is an **upper-bound** reading (assuming that what was reported is the best).
4. **Column-name convention**: this table accepts both the new Ultralytics spelling `metrics/mAP50-95(B)` and the old YOLOv5/v3 spelling
   `metrics/mAP_0.5:0.95` (each row marks which one it uses). The two spellings denote the same quantity, but the **column names differ**——
   any script that "filters logs by column name" will miss the other branch; that is the purpose of this item: to make the convention difference visible.
```

---

## S6 · Process protocols (shared tooling; used by both papers, neither claims originality)

> **Purpose**: main text §2.3 states that "this paper and its companion paper share the same set of process protocols, and neither claims originality".
> This section **grounds that statement in verifiable names and hashes** (this is how cross-citation rule ⑤b is implemented).
> **Form decision (this paper's side)**: these two protocols are **project-internal artifacts**, and **their text is not released with this paper**——they are not public publications,
> and they bear no direct relation to this paper's scientific claims. This paper states only that "**the same set of protocols was used**", and gives the **file names and md5**,
> so that a verifier can request the same files from the authors. **If a reviewer requires it, they can be supplied together with the revision.**

| Protocol (internal artifact) | md5 | Role in this paper | Claims originality? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `the multi-model review checklist (shared protocol)` | `e4c73a5ca5992b7af6a5a66c84ba4561` | Stratified review by external models; among the ten self-reported defects in §10, "silent exclusion" and "wrong column taken" were found by that review | **No**, neither paper claims it |
| `the local/cloud working-method guide (shared protocol)` | `10d1b3a3e21f018f3b82e4a293242695` | Local/cloud working method: scripted remote execution, all-or-nothing patches, audit anchors, a local pointer for every number | **No**, neither paper claims it |

**The same convention also applies to the audit-anchor system**: the 289 anchors of this paper and the 494 anchors of the companion paper are the **same** shared tooling,
and neither paper presents it as a methodological contribution.

**Why the text is not attached**: ① both are **Chinese working documents** with no direct relation to this paper's scientific claims;
② attaching them would make "process" read as "methodological contribution", which is exactly what this rule is meant to avoid; ③ giving the md5 is already enough to confirm that "the two papers use the same one".

---

## S7 · Reproduction entry points (anyone can recompute independently)

```powershell
# ① Main-text anchors (289 entries; must ALL PASS)
python work\audit_anchors_fupaper.py

# ② 2×2 matrix analysis of S4 (reads the artifacts pulled back in this round)
python work\xeval_analyze_20260916.py

# ③ Mechanism check of §5.6 (reads the D: archive, read-only)
python work\gap_mechanism_20260916.py

# ④ Integrity of the artifacts pulled back in this round (sha256 + byte counts)
python work\verify_pulled.py
python work\verify_pulled.py _xframe
```

**Convention statement**: the source files of S1–S7 are all different and **must not be mixed**——S1/S2 are a **configuration-level audit** (no datasets are downloaded),
S3 is a **targeted sampling of official configs/scripts** (5 frameworks, not a census), while S4/S5 are **empirical** (the former on our own runs, the latter on other people's logs).
The strength of every statement in the main text is distinguished accordingly.

> **Two notes spanning S24–S27 (added 2026-09-18).** ① The experiment contains
> **90 runs**: three registered pairs 3 × 2 × 10 = 60, the saturated control 2 × 5 = 10
> (the registered set is 70), and the control appended by amendment 2 × 10 = 20 — counted
> from the run directories, ten seeds per arm in every cell but the saturated control.
> ② **Val-side alias bias**, `Δ ≈ Δ_reported + d̄` with `d̄ = mean[p_baseline − p_strategy]`
> and `p = max_e − final` from each run’s own log: T1-a **+0.051** [0.006, 0.099], T1-c
> **+0.214** [0.086, 0.326], control C **+0.406** [0.313, 0.498], saturated control
> **+0.961** [0.440, 1.674] (Δ + d̄ = −0.529). Its decomposition: baseline arm **2.505 pp**, strategy arm **1.543 pp** — which is a **0.616 reading** of that cell’s −1.490 pp (1.543 / 2.505 ≈ three-fifths, **not** two-thirds). T1-b is excluded by construction — its YAML
> separates the keys. Script `work/x10_alias_audit_20260918.py`. **No verdict changes.**

> **`T1-d`'s `val` side, now complete (2026-10-02).** The registered pair `dota->dota15` carried `n = 7` on its
> `val` side because the archived batch had no per-epoch curve for its last three seeds; those runs'
> `results.csv` were still in the training machine's run directories, and the three curves now ship
> (`02_release_data/t1d_valext_20261002/`), with `work/p2_t1d_val_n10_20261002.py` reproducing the seven
> previously published per-run values **bit-exactly** as its control. At `n = 10` the `val`-side arm difference
> is **-0.4056 pp**, 95 % CI **[-0.5175, -0.2937]**, paired t **-8.201**, **p = 1.8x10^-5**, **0+/10-** (at
> `n = 7`: -0.4043 pp, p = 0.00106): the point estimate is unchanged and the interval tightens. The article's
> Declarations no longer lists this input among the non-recomputable ones. **No verdict changes.**

### Table S9 — moved from *5.6 Why Δgap's sign varies by corpus: three of five candidate mechanisms are refuted by the data*

**Table S9.** *5.6 Why Δgap's sign varies by corpus: three of five candidate mechanisms are refuted by the data* — columns: Candidate mechanism, Prediction, Verdict, Refutation / evidence.

| Candidate mechanism | Prediction | Verdict | Refutation / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M1 multiplicative gain** | if the strategy improves by a fixed **ratio**, Δgap has the same sign as the raw gap | **refuted** | 3 counterexamples: `mask20` (gap −31.2 but Δgap +1.75), `p_aitovis` (+1.03 but −1.23), `p_vistod15` (+5.34 but −3.32) |
| **M2 ceiling compression** | the easier `val` is (larger \|gap\|), the more the strategy's advantage is compressed, so Δgap turns negative as \|gap\| grows | **refuted** | all six cells with \|gap\| > 10 have Δgap **positive** (+0.35 to +13.66); overall r = +0.32, p = 0.28 |
| **M3 level determines resolvability** | cells with higher mAP are more likely to be significant | **not supported** | significant cells' baseline val mean **36.83 pp**, non-significant **36.79 pp** (Welch p = 0.998) |
| **M4 whether the strategy truly gains on test** | only cells where the strategy gains on test can show `val` understating | **refuted** | 2 counterexamples: `p_aitovis` and `p_vistod15` are better on test yet have **negative** Δgap |
| **M5 difference in val-side selection gain** | Δgap has the same sign as (premium_baseline − premium_strategy) | **not supported** | only **8 of 13** cells share the sign; r = +0.06, p = 0.84 |

|---|---|---|
| 14/19 (the reported layer) | **12/19** | (h-1) removes D-Fire; DIOR and Mendeley are evidence-insufficient at the reported layer |
| 5/19 clean | **3/19** | the same two rows move to "undecidable" (they were never adjudicated), so the clean group loses two |
| 12/19 (called "release/spec layer") | **release 10/19, protocol 13/19** | **12 is neither**: it was obtained by a **two-row manual adjustment** (removing WIDER FACE and CrowdHuman), not by applying one unit consistently to all 19 rows |

### Table S14 — moved from *6.5 Across frameworks: is the convention specific to YOLO?*

**Table S14.** *6.5 Across frameworks: is the convention specific to YOLO?* — columns: Framework (pinned commit), Split the reported metric comes from, Also used for selection?, Status of an independent "test".

| Framework (pinned commit) | Split the reported metric comes from | Also used for selection? | Status of an independent "test" |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Ultralytics YOLO** [16] (the `yolo_dist` unit of §6.1) | the YAML's `val:` | **yes** (`best.pt`; §5's "selection premium" is defined on exactly this convention) | the config **has** a `test:` key, but **2 of 19 rows bind it to the same path** (§6.1) |
| **Detectron2** [30] `a2f4a8771ab7` | `DATASETS.TEST: ("coco_2017_val",)` | **no** (`TEST.EVAL_PERIOD = 0`, no periodic evaluation by default) | `coco_2017_test` exists, but its annotation file is **`image_info_test2017.json` — image information only, no ground truth** |
| **MMDetection** [31] `cfd5d3a985b0` | `CocoMetric(ann_file='annotations/instances_val2017.json')`, `val_interval=1` | **no** (`CheckpointHook(interval=1)`, the base config has **no** `save_best`) | **`test_dataloader = val_dataloader` and `test_evaluator = val_evaluator`** (`configs/_base_/datasets/coco_detection.py`, lines 65 and 73) — the `test:` key **exists but is the same object as `val`**, so there is **no independent test split**; an earlier reading of this row said the key was absent, which that file contradicts |
| **YOLOX** [29] `6ddff4824372` | `val_ann = "instances_val2017.json"` | **yes** (`trainer.py` tracks `self.best_ap` and saves the best) | `test_ann` is used **only when `testdev=True`** |
| **PaddleDetection** [33] `b25522a0f4bd` | `EvalDataset` = `val2017` | **not written by default, and now provable line by line** — `paddledetection/tools__train.py` declares `--eval` as `action='store_true', default=False` and passes it to `trainer.train(FLAGS.eval)`; the assignment `status['save_best_model'] = True` sits inside the validation branch of `paddledetection/ppdet__engine__trainer.py` (`if validate and is_snapshot:`), and `best_model` is written only through `paddledetection/ppdet__engine__callbacks.py`'s gate `if 'save_best_model' in status and status['save_best_model']:` — both files are now in the SHA-256 list | **`TestDataset` reads an annotation-free `ImageFolder`, with `anno_path` written as `annotations/instances_val2017.json`** |
| **DETR** [32] `29901c51d7fe` | `build_dataset(image_set='val')` | **no** (evaluates every epoch but does not track the best) | the `PATHS` in `datasets/coco.py` have **only train and val, no test key** |

> **Correction (2026-10-04, and a defect of our instrument).** The MMDetection row above was first written as "the base config has **no** test split at all". That is **false**: at the pinned commit, `configs/_base_/datasets/coco_detection.py` lines **65** and **73** read `test_dataloader = val_dataloader` and `test_evaluator = val_evaluator`. The extraction behind this table (`work/analyze_xframe_20260916.py`) searched only for `val_*` keys, so the `test_*` keys were **absent from the evidence file rather than absent from the config**. The corrected reading is **stronger for the article's claim**: the key exists but is bound to the validation object — an **alias, not an independent split**. On the same day the other rows were re-checked against the pinned sources and stand (YOLOX: `test_ann` used only when `testdev=True`, `yolox/exp/yolox_base.py` 301/306/307; PaddleDetection: `if validate and is_snapshot:` in `ppdet/engine/trainer.py` 694 with `status['save_best_model'] = True` at 721).

### Table S19 — moved from *8.4 The three variance components: the excluded one is the smaller in this sample*

**Table S19.** *8.4 The three variance components: the excluded one is the smaller in this sample* — columns: Source, What was varied, Magnitude.

| Source | What was varied | Magnitude |
|---|---|---|
| augmentation RNG | 3 augmentation seeds | 0.246 pp (strategy) / 0.190 pp (baseline) |
| **initialization (source-side retraining from scratch)** | 3 → **5** independent initializations | **0.146 → 0.439 pp (gain) / 0.209 → 0.253 pp (absolute endpoint)** |
| second-stage data order | 9–10 seeds | 0.403 pp (smoke) / **0.515 pp (SHWD)** |

> **The three rows are not in one unit, and we do not compare them as if they were.** The augmentation and
> data-order rows give **standard deviations of the run-to-run change (pp)**; the initialization row is
> reported in **both** of the units its source record carries — **0.146 pp as a gain** and **0.209 pp as an
> absolute endpoint** — because those two are not interchangeable. The claim §8.4 makes is a **ranking**,
> and that ranking holds under either reading; the three magnitudes are **not** comparable across rows in a
> single unit, and **no cross-row arithmetic is performed on them**.

> **The initialization row was extended on 2026-09-30 (five initializations), and the extension weakens
> the reading rather than confirming it.** Two further source-side retrainings (data-order seeds 45 and
> 46, augmentation seeds 404 and 505) give gains of **+0.430** and **+1.390 pp**; with them the gain SD
> moves **0.146 → 0.439 pp** (mean **+0.626**, range **1.110**) and the absolute-endpoint SD
> **0.209 → 0.253 pp** (43.070–43.630). The bootstrap 95 % interval for the five-initialization gain SD is
> **[0.058, 0.580]**, which **overlaps** the data-order row's **0.488 pp** (n = 10; 0.515 pp at n = 9) — so
> "the excluded component is the smaller" holds **in the three-initialization sample only**, and this
> supplement reports the ranking as sample-scoped rather than established. The same run reproduces the
> archived three initializations value by value (43.630/+0.570, 43.590/+0.280, 43.250/+0.460, max
> |Δ| = **0.005 pp**). Script: `work/G3_sigma_n5_20260930.py`.

### Table S20 — moved from *8.6 ⭐ The premium's realization rate: **not significant at claim level**; arm-cell median **17.6 %** (five arm-cells, n = 10; run-weighted **23.3 %**)*

**Table S20.** *8.6 ⭐ The premium's realization rate: **not significant at claim level**; arm-cell median 17.6 % (five arm-cells, n = 10; run-weighted 23.3 %)* — columns: Cell, Arm, n, `prem_val`, `prem_test`, realization rate.

> **Which cells are these five, and why exactly five.** The rule is mechanical: **every arm-cell whose `test` side had reached n = 10 at the 2026-09-25 freeze** — both arms of `shwd2sf` and of `smoke2sf`, plus `aitod20` **baseline** (50 runs); `work/item2_final_v2_20260925.py` applies it and prints the pool, the per-cell means and the **23.26 %** aggregate. The `aitod20` **strategy** arm finished after the freeze: it is **the sixth row of Table 4, printed as Table S20c below**, **kept out of this pool** (feeding it in gives **8.7 %**), and **its exclusion was decided after its readings were in, not by a pre-registered rule**. **Table S20 and Table 4 therefore print five and six cells on purpose.**

| Cell | Arm | n | `prem_val` | **`prem_test`** | **realization rate** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| shwd2sf | baseline | 10 | +1.469 | **+0.736** | **50 %** |
| shwd2sf | strategy | 10 | +0.865 | **+0.152** | **18 %** |
| smoke2sf | baseline | 10 | +0.691 | **+0.509** | **74 %** |
| smoke2sf | strategy | 10 | +0.605 | **−0.157** | **−26 %** |
| aitod20 | baseline | 10 | +0.766 | **−0.218** | **−28 %** |


> **The same five cells at one decimal place**, so the median can be re-derived rather than taken on
> trust: 50.1 %, 17.6 %, 73.7 %, -26.0 %, -28.5 % **⇒ median 17.6 %** -- the figure the article,
> Table 4 and this table print. The whole-number rates above are the same ratios rounded for display;
> recomputing the median from *those* is what makes it look inconsistent.
> **One earlier reading of this same cell is left standing, on purpose.** The verbatim analysis dump
> in §S4 still shows `aitod20 base 1 … −0.377` — that is **this cell before it was completed**, at
> n = 1, and §S4 is that sample's record, not a competing summary; the row above is the completed
> n = 10 measurement. §S4 keeps its original bytes and its recorded hash because it is an embedded
> dated artifact.

> **A theory-driven prediction, tested on the same runs (added later).** §3.1 derives
> realization rate = 1 − WC / `prem_val` with WC := E[ε_sel − ε_final] ≥ 0, the winner's-curse term.
> On the archive: ① WC = `prem_val` − `prem_test` = **+0.463 pp** per run (t = 8.34, p = 1.8×10⁻¹²;
> the realized premium is smaller in **68/81** runs); ② sorting runs by the noise scale σ̂ of their
> `val` curve gives realization-rate terciles of **65.6 % / 49.8 % / 13.8 %**, **ρ = −0.466** (p = 1.3×10⁻⁵, n = 80). **Both readings are re-taken with arm-cell clustering, as the kappa fit already is: WC = +0.463 pp with a cluster-robust 95 % interval [+0.204, +0.721] and p = 0.0033, and ρ = −0.466 with a cluster-bootstrap interval [−0.651, −0.131] and p = 0.0115 — which at the cluster level (n = 9) is −0.433 with p = 0.244 — so the clustered reading is the one this paper reports (Supplementary S12, section 8.6, script work/r102_cluster_20260927.py).** The stronger form (premium ≈ σ√(2 ln E)) was **withdrawn**:
> slope 0.026 ± 0.222, p = 0.82, n = 101. Scripts: `work/theory_p4_test_20260919.py`,
> `work/theory_meta_and_tests_20260919.py`.

### Table S38 — the selection term, cell by cell: `prem_test` and WC for all thirteen scanned corpora

**Table S38.** *5.6 / 8.6* — the selection contribution proper, `prem_val − prem_test` = WC, formed here for **every** scanned corpus and both arms. The five arm-cells printed in Table S20 are repeated with that table's values; the other twenty-one were closed on **2026-09-30** by scoring **every archived 5-epoch checkpoint** on each run's own held-out `test` split: **1,568 grid checkpoints over 140 runs** — the two figures are a **decomposition, not two counts of the same thing**: **1,848 evaluations in total = 1,568 grid checkpoints + 140 runs × 2 endpoints (`best`/`last`)**, on one machine, with the pipeline's registered call (`YOLO(w)` → `val(split="test", batch=32, imgsz=640, plots=False, save_json=False)`, no loss re-import), the data YAML read from each run's own `args.yaml` and asserted to be three-way with a `test:` split. `n` is the number of seeds carrying a `test` curve for that arm (3 where the scan ran at n = 3, 10 where the cell was later escalated, and 10 for the five arm-cells of Table S20); `prem_val` and `prem_test` are means over those seeds, in pp, with `prem_test = T_best − T_final`; realization rate = `prem_test / prem_val`. **The same run reproduces the values printed earlier in this supplement**: over the 21 arm-cells carrying both readings, the val maximum, `prem_val` and the archived `T_best` agree to **max |Δ| = 0.005 pp**, which is the printed rounding (`work/g6_repro_vs_printed_20260930.py`); the coverage audit reports **140/140 runs, 1,848 rows, 0 anomalies** (`g6_prem_per_run.csv` and `g6_perepoch_test_curves.csv`). Per-run readings and the curves themselves: release files `g6_prem_per_run.csv` and `g6_perepoch_test_curves.csv`.

| shwd2sf | strategy | 10 | +0.865 | **+0.152** | +0.713 | **18 %** |

> **What this table does, and what it does not do.** It closes a **verifiability** gap: the ten corpora
> and the `aitod20` strategy arm previously carried no `test` curve at all, so `prem_test`, and hence
> WC, could not be formed for them — any reader can now recompute every WC above from the released
> per-run file. It does **not** upgrade a claim: five of these arm-cells sit at n = 3, and §7.1's power
> argument is unchanged. Read descriptively, the asymmetry the five arm-cells of §8.6 show is the
> general case here: **WC > 0 in 25 of the 26 arm-cells**, the **arm-cell median realization rate is
> 17.2 %** (mean 8.9 %, ten of the 26 negative), and the realized premium is **negative in 8 of the 13
> strategy arms** (and in 2 of the 13 baseline arms). Scripts: `work/g6_prem_analyze_20260930.py`,
> `work/g6_repro_vs_printed_20260930.py`; the evaluation itself: `work/g6_perepoch_eval.py`,
> `work/g6_coverage_audit.py`.

### Table S40b — the three intervention axes side by side

**Table S40b.** *the three intervention axes, cell by cell* — the estimand is the article's Table 2 one,
`Δgap = (M_val(reference) − M_val(treatment)) − (M_test(reference) − M_test(treatment))`, paired by seed
inside a cell and computed on one machine per cell; the `lr0` rows are article Table 2's, the other two
axes are Tables S39 and S40. `p` is that axis' sign-flip permutation *p* of Δgap, except on the `lr0`
rows, where it is the paired *t* *p* article Table 2 prints (that table's own convention); every *p* is
raw and uncorrected. "Arm difference created" is the axis' own paired gain on the endpoint: an axis that
moves the endpoint by no more than seed noise cannot say anything about whether the shift reproduces.

| Axis (reference → treatment) | Cell | Δgap (pp) | *p* | direction | arm difference created | reproduces the corpus-dependent direction |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`lr0` 0.001 → 0.005** (headline axis) | shwd2sf | **+0.779** | 0.0053 | `val` understates | **yes** (article Table 1) | **yes** — the four cells split 2 understate / 2 overstate |
| | smoke2sf | **+0.461** | 0.0263 | `val` understates | **yes** | |
| | p_aitovis | **−1.226** | 1.3×10⁻⁸ | `val` overstates | **yes** | |
| | p_vistod15 | **−3.315** | 6.5×10⁻⁹ | `val` overstates | **yes** | |
| `weight_decay` 1e-4 → 1e-3 | smoke2sf | −0.009 | 0.9648 | 6+/4− | **no** (+0.020 pp, *p* = 0.8535) | **uninformative** |
| | shwd2sf | +0.190 | 0.3066 | 7+/3− | **no** (+0.090 pp, *p* = 0.582) | **uninformative** |
| `mosaic` 1.0 → 0.0 | smoke2sf | +0.035 | 0.8242 | 6+/4− | **yes** (−1.135 pp, *p* = 0.001953) | no |
| | shwd2sf | **−0.520** | **0.0293** | **2+/8−** | **yes** (−0.699 pp, *p* = 0.001953) | **yes** — one cell at zero, one significantly negative |

**Which axis reproduces, and which one cannot say.** Of the two intervention axes run besides the headline
`lr0` one, `weight_decay` **never produced a comparable arm difference** — its own paired gain is +0.020
and +0.090 pp, the same order as seed noise — so its non-reproduction is **uninformative**, and the paper
does not read "the phenomenon fails to cross axes" off it. `mosaic` did produce one (**0+/10−** in both
cells, −1.135 and −0.699 pp) and its Δgap has the **same form as the `lr0` axis'**: one cell at zero, one
significantly negative. The systematic shift is therefore **not specific to the learning-rate axis**, which
is what section 12 of the article states and what this table now lets a reader check in one place.

### Table S25 — moved from *§9: the registered replication (the criterion is not met — the third row is the one that passes)*

**Table S25.** *§9: the registered replication (the criterion is not met — the third row is the one that passes)* — columns: Question the frozen criterion asks, Measured.

| Question the frozen criterion asks | Measured |
|---|---|
| \|Δ\| < 0.15 pp? (a saturated domain should be ≈ 0) | **no** (T2 mask→mendeley **−1.490 pp**) |
| p > 0.1? (should be non-significant) | **no** (paired t = −4.164, **p = 0.0141**, 0+/5−) |
| A **positive** gain ≥ +0.30 pp with p < 0.01? (if present, the narrative collapses) | **no** |

**Endpoint and floor (added 2026-10-01; corrected 2026-10-04).** Every value in the Measured column above is the
**`final`-epoch** reading of the archive-A row (§7.3, Table S16). The frozen endpoint convention of §5 is
the **`best`** reading, and the **released** `test_best_epoch` entry for this cell read **Δ = −0.902 pp**
(**p = 0.2931**); that cell was corrected on **2026-10-02** (§S12): the entry had used one run's **`last`** reading in
place of its `best` reading, and the corrected value is **Δ = −1.490 pp, p = 0.0141**. **So all three
questions get the same answer under both endpoints** (|−1.490| ≫ 0.15 pp; p ≪ 0.1; a negative reading
cannot be a positive gain): the table is **endpoint-independent** and the criterion **not met** either way. The p values here come from the paired **t** test; at n = 5 the
permutation floor is **2/2⁵ = 0.0625**, which is *above* the printed p = 0.0141 and is printed here as §11
recommendation ③ requires.

> **The criterion was fixed before the runs existed**: a ten-seed paired difference failing to be
> positive with **≥ 8/10 same-sign** and a **95 % CI excluding zero** would remove `dota15` from the
> four-cell headline and force the abstract and Tier 1 to be rewritten. Measured: **+0.449 pp**,
> **10+/0−**, CI **[+0.309, +0.589]** — met as stated.
>
> **Recipe identity.** All twenty `args.yaml` files of the cell (the sixteen new runs plus the two
> pre-existing seeds, both arms) are identical in every recipe field: same pretrain
> (`dota15_pretrain.pt`), same data YAML (`dota15_20p.yaml`), 100 epochs, `patience` 100, batch 32,
> imgsz 640, `save_period` 5, SGD, `lr0` 0.001 (baseline) versus 0.005 (strategy). Old and new runs
> are therefore poolable, and the seed is recorded in every directory name.
>
> **GPU-memory fallback, and its sensitivity.** 10 of the 16 newly trained runs printed
> `CUDA OutOfMemoryError in TaskAlignedAssigner, using CPU` (92 events; 79 of them in
> `dota15_lr005_100ep_s51n`), while the per-epoch sweep of §8.7 shared the GPU. This is an
> implementation-level fallback inside one batch, not a change of objective, and it is reported
> rather than smoothed: dropping each seed pair in turn leaves the criterion satisfied in all ten
> subsets (worst lower bound **+0.277**), and dropping the two most affected pairs (s51: 79 events,
> s42: 7) gives **+0.428 pp**, CI **[+0.252, +0.605]**, 8+/0−. Scripts:
> `work/x8_dota15_analysis_20260918.py`, `work/x8_oom_counts_20260918.py`,
> `work/x8_oom_sensitivity_20260918.py`.

### Table S29 — moved from *5.1 Direction: reported in two tiers, and the second tier's sign varies by corpus*

**Table S29.** *5.1 (the two `val`-overstating cells, X1)* — columns: seed, Δgap for `p_aitovis`, Δgap for `p_vistod15`. Ten seeds per cell, from n = 3 to n = 10. Δgap = (arm difference on `val`, at each run's own val-curve maximum) − (arm difference on `test`, at `best.pt`); paired within seed. Sources: `x1_tier2_20260918/runs/` (40 run records) and `teval.csv` (80 test evaluations, SHA-256 verified against the machine that produced them).

| seed | Δgap `p_aitovis` | Δgap `p_vistod15` |
|---|---|---|
| 42 | -1.127 | -3.131 |
| 43 | -0.843 | -4.563 |
| 44 | -1.358 | -3.058 |
| 45 | -1.495 | -2.819 |
| 46 | -1.267 | -3.468 |
| 47 | -0.960 | -3.453 |
| 48 | -1.280 | -2.952 |
| 49 | -1.341 | -3.590 |
| 50 | -1.404 | -2.995 |
| 51 | -1.182 | -3.118 |
| **mean (n = 10)** | **-1.226** | **-3.315** |
| 95 % CI | [-1.370, -1.081] | [-3.676, -2.953] |
| paired t / p | -19.17 / 1.3e-08 | -20.76 / 6.5e-09 |
| sign | 0+/10− | 0+/10− |
| **sign-flip permutation p** | **0.001953** | **0.001953** |

The `paired t / p` row is **raw** (no multiplicity adjustment); the permutation p is exact at this cell size and is printed as its attainable floor. The two are separate tests and are not combined.

> **What the comparison is, exactly.** The plan fixed before the runs: 40 training runs
> (seeds 42–51 × 2 arms × 2 cells), paired *and* sign-flip tests, `{best, last} × {val, test}`, and a
> prespecified correction; the strong branch of the pre-declared outcome rule was *"both remaining
> negative and significant with permutation p ≤ 0.002"*, the weak branch being that the abstract's
> "direction is not single-valued" must be withdrawn or limited. All ten seeds of each cell are
> negative and the permutation p is 2/2¹⁰ = **0.001953**, the smallest value attainable at n = 10
> (§7.1), so the strong branch applies. **Holm across the two targets** (the prespecified
> correction) multiplies the larger p by 2 and the smaller by 1: 2.6×10⁻⁸ and 6.5×10⁻⁹, both ≤ 0.05.
>
> **The endpoint was identified, not assumed.** The archived `sio_b_results.csv` that the published
> Table 2 used for the n = 3 values carries no endpoint convention (§7.3, disclosed defect 3). Our
> own evaluation of `best.pt` on `test` reproduces its values for the twelve runs it covers to
> **0.0046 pp** (median 0.0023) — so that archive records **the best checkpoint evaluated on `test`**,
> and the n = 3 → n = 10 comparison is like-for-like rather than a change of measurement. Under the
> same construction restricted to seeds 42–44 the two cells give −1.110 and −3.584 against the
> published −1.114 and −3.580.
>
> **Both arms and all four quantities.** Per cell (baseline / strategy means, n = 10): `val`-curve
> maximum 11.242 / 12.657 and 16.963 / 23.927; `best.pt` on `test` 10.210 / 10.396 and 11.626 / 15.263.
> The cells' realization rates (§8.6's quantity, computed the same way) are **82 % / 104 %** and
> **56 % / 29 %**, pooled **55 %** — the opposite end from §8.6's aggregate realization rate, which is why the article says
> the realization rate is not a constant across corpora. The exact three-component decomposition of
> each Δgap is in Tables S10's conventions: for `p_aitovis` −1.226 = (−1.583) + (+0.090) + (+0.267)
> and for `p_vistod15` −3.315 = (−7.072) + (−0.213) + (+3.971); both identities check to the printed
> digit. Scripts: `work/x1_gap_analysis_20260918.py`, `work/x1_aux_20260918.py`.

### Table S37 — moved from *3.2 Six experiments, five judged criteria, on GPU machines, each with its criterion written down first*

**Table S37.** *3.2* — the six pre-registered experiments and the criterion each was judged against. **G1 is void** (design confounded), so no verdict is entered for it; **two of the five judged experiments are negative — G1′ and G2** — and one design had to be redone, all reported as such. Counts are over experiments, not criteria. The values behind every verdict are in Tables S31–S33 (the G1 family in S12).

| Experiment (design) | Pre-declared criterion | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **G1** aliasing calibration, disjoint split | none entered: design confounded | **void** — the pair differed by 14.7 pp in difficulty; redone as G1′ (§S12) |
| **G1″** three-way carve levels | the paired levels differ by ≤ 3 pp | **met** (−0.122 pp; §S12) |
| **G1′** aliasing, level-matched pair | WC > 0, paired t, p < 0.05 | **not met** (p = 0.83, n = 10; §S12) |
| **G2** selection-split size, 30 vs 90 | the rate rises with size; `WC_small/WC_large` ∈ [1.4, 2.1] | **direction met, magnitude not met** (pooled ratio 1.19; Table S31) |
| **G3** epoch budget `E` | C1 rise in `E`; C2 the σ√(2 ln E) form in [0.86, 1.43]; C3 shape; C4 WC on a disjoint readout | **revised** (§3.1, S32 · S34b, **clean re-run**): C1 met, C2 out of band, so the scaling stays withdrawn; C3 passes on the mean only; C4 not met |
| **G4** third corpus, `mende20_3way` | identity value by value; WC > 0 | **met** (WC = +5.21 pp, CI [+4.82, +5.66], 20/20; Table S33) |

Magnitude: `WC_small / WC_large` within [1.4, 2.1] (√3 ≈ 1.73) — **not met** (measured 1.19, pooled over the two arms), so the 1/√n step is a scale estimate, not a law.


### Table S32 — moved from *3.2 Six experiments, five judged criteria, on GPU machines, each with its criterion written down first*

**Table S32.** *3.2 (G3)* — the epoch budget is the only thing that changes (four budgets, E = 50–400, same corpus, recipe and validation split).  Columns: arm, n, premium at E = 50, at E = 100, at E = 200, at E = 400, ratio 400/100.  Source: `g3_epochs_runs/`. **Two bases appear in this table and its note, and they are labelled:** the E = 400 and ratio columns report the **six full runs** at that budget, while the C2 ratio **1.329** and the all-runs C1 verdict use the **ten runs** at E = 400 (arm-equal mean **+0.907 pp**); four of the ten stopped early under the protocol's own `patience`. **n = 5 seeds per arm at E = 50–200**; the E = 400 and ratio columns report the **six full runs** of the ten at that budget, which is why the table row and the note's all-ten arithmetic are both stated.

| arm | n | premium (E = 50) | premium (E = 100) | premium (E = 200) | premium (E = 400; **six full runs**) | ratio 400/100 (**six full runs**) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| base | 5 | +0.724 | +0.897 | +1.187 | +1.315 | ×1.47 |
| lr005 | 5 | +0.163 | +0.468 | +0.756 | +1.241 | ×2.65 |

> **Pre-declared criteria (C1–C3, fixed before the runs).** **C1 — monotone rise of the premium with E — is not met**: **on all ten runs** the four-budget series peaks at E = 200 and falls at E = 400 (four of the ten E = 400 runs stopped early at effective budgets 388/318/362/375; on the six full runs the E = 400 mean is +1.278 pp, which is the column this table prints). The ln E regression over E = 50–200 gives slope **+0.3990** (se 0.0592), **p = 9.466e-08**. **C2 — the σ√(2 ln E) form — is not rejected**: premium(400)/premium(100) = **1.329** (the E = 400 arm-equal mean over all ten runs is **+0.907 pp**) against the predicted 1.141, **inside** the pre-declared band [0.86, 1.43]. **C3 — the shape control — passes** (ê/E 0.833 → 0.815). The extension (E = 50 and 400) was added for this revision; it uses the same corpus, recipe, seeds and arms as the E = 100/200 runs, and only `--epochs` differs. **The early-stopped runs decide the outcome**: the four E = 400 runs that stopped under the protocol’s own `patience` (effective budgets 388/318/362/375) are what makes the series non-monotone; dropping them flips **both** criteria — the six full E = 400 runs run to a mean of +1.278 pp on a monotone series (C1 met) and move the ratio outside the band (C2 rejected). Both readings are conditional on that restriction and are reported as such; the pre-declared verdict above is the one computed on **all** runs. **This note supersedes an earlier reading of the two-budget series, which reported only three budgets and mislabelled them as monotone; the four-budget verdict is in S11 and §3.2. The table below is the with-early-stopping reading; the same four budgets re-run **on one machine** with early stopping disabled **satisfy C1**, put **C2 outside its band** and **pass C3 on the pre-declared mean reading** (only the lr005 arm exceeds it) — see Table S34b.**

**Secondary criterion C4 (WC on a disjoint readout) — not met, and systematically so.** With ê selected on `val` and read out on the disjoint third C3 (100 images; `C3 ∩ val = 0` and `C3 ∩ train = 0` verified programmatically), WC = `prem_val` − `prem_C3` is **negative in all 16 readouts** (mean **-0.616 pp**): the peak-to-endpoint gap is *larger* on the small disjoint split than on `val`. That is consistent with the shape reading above — the premium is largely the split's own curve shape, and a 100-image split has a steeper curve — and it means Proposition 4's inequality **cannot be tested by reading out on a small disjoint carve**. Two limitations are stated with the result: the readout split has 100 images only, and the four early-stopped E = 400 runs are excluded, leaving n = 6 at that budget.

### Table S34 — cross-lineage realization test (non-YOLO detector)

**Table S34.** *6.5* — the mechanism outside the YOLO lineage. A **Faster R-CNN (ResNet-50 FPN)** detector
(torchvision `fasterrcnn_resnet50_fpn`), a family that **reports the final epoch by default** (the
cross-framework audit above), trained from COCO-initialised weights on the same **three-way protocol** as
the rest of this paper: train = a 20 % subset (1,294 images), selection (`val`) = a **verified-disjoint
carve** (500), readout (`test`) = the corpus's held-out split (1,610). 40 epochs, batch 8, SGD lr 0.005
with a 1,000-iteration warmup, 640-px input, **six seeds**. `best` is chosen by the **`val`** curve's
argmax; `test` is inferred **once** for `best` and once for `last`, and evaluated with the
**same** COCO implementation used on the YOLO side (IoU 0.50:0.95, 101-point interpolation).
Source: `nonyolo_realization.json` (the original run; **author-side, not part of the released package**) and `nonyolo_settle_20260925/` (`settle_all.json`,
`nonyolo_n6_aggregate.json`; the five added seeds are aggregated by `work/aggregate_nonyolo_n6_20260925.py`).
The per-epoch `val` predictions kept by the training run are written one file per epoch under the run's prediction directory, from epoch 1 to epoch 40.

| 1000 | 6 | +0.0809 | +0.0551 | 68.2 % | [26.9 %, 123.5 %] |

rather than patched. **40/40 runs completed**, each to its configured budget. The E = 50/100/200 rows
coincide with the published budget series because no run early-stopped at those budgets; only E = 400
changes.

| 50 | base | 5 | **+0.724** | 0.094 | [+0.663, +0.806] | 0.760 |

| 100 | base | 5 | **+0.897** | 0.101 | [+0.820, +0.974] | 0.788 |
| 100 | lr005 | 5 | **+0.468** | 0.220 | [+0.290, +0.633] | 0.878 |
| 200 | base | 5 | **+1.187** | 0.084 | [+1.119, +1.250] | 0.813 |
| 200 | lr005 | 5 | **+0.756** | 0.163 | [+0.628, +0.900] | 0.909 |
| 400 | base | 5 | **+1.295** | 0.083 | [+1.222, +1.349] | 0.760 |
| 400 | lr005 | 5 | **+1.248** | 0.085 | [+1.180, +1.317] | 0.814 |

> **Verdict under the pre-declared criteria, on the single-machine series.** The arm-equal means are
> **+0.4439 / +0.6824 / +0.9712 / +1.2717**, i.e. **monotone non-decreasing ⇒ C1 is *met*** (per-run
> regression on ln E: slope **+0.3999**, se **0.0503**, t = **7.950**, p = **1.325×10⁻⁹**, n = 40),
> where the with-early-stopping reading scored **not met**. **C2 is out of band**:
> premium(400)/premium(100) = **1.8636** against the predicted 1.141, outside the pre-declared
> [0.86, 1.43] ⇒ the σ√(2 ln E) scaling **stays withdrawn** and the level reads as shape-driven.
> **C3 — the shape control — passes on the pre-declared *mean* reading** (ê/E **0.833 → 0.787**,
> |Δ| = **0.046** ≤ 0.05) and **fails per arm** (lr005 |Δ| = **0.065**; base |Δ| = **0.028**); the
> pre-registered criterion names the mean, so both readings are reported side by side and neither is
> dropped. **The registered criteria still forbid reporting the rise as an `E` effect**: the peak's
> relative position moves **in one arm and not the other** across budgets (base 0.760 → 0.760, lr005
> 0.876 → 0.814), which is the movement C3 exists to catch.

> **Superseded reading, kept on the record (cross-machine batch).** The first clean batch carried only
> E = 50 and E = 400 and was split across two hosts; its per-arm values were

| `E` | arm | n | premium mean (pp) | seed sd | bootstrap 95 % CI | `ê/E` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 50 | base | 5 | **+0.672** | 0.180 | [+0.526, +0.820] | 0.744 |
| 50 | lr005 | 5 | +0.094 | 0.110 | [+0.013, +0.185] | 0.928 |
| 400 | base | 5 | **+1.298** | 0.082 | [+1.223, +1.343] | 0.836 |
| 400 | lr005 | 5 | **+1.211** | 0.284 | [+1.058, +1.469] | 0.725 |

> and its verdict read: *using the published E = 100 and 200 means (0.682, 0.971), the four-budget
> series is **monotone** (0.383 / 0.682 / 0.971 / **1.255**) and the ln E regression gives slope
> **+0.419** (se 0.060), **t = 7.03, p ≈ 2×10⁻¹²** ⇒ the monotone-rise criterion **met**, where the
> with-early-stopping readings scored **not met**. The **shape control fails**: `max_arm |ê/E(400) −
> ê/E(50)| = 0.203` against the pre-declared 0.05, and the movement is **opposite in the two arms**
> (lr005 falls 0.928 → 0.725, base rises 0.744 → 0.836).* Those values are **replaced** by the
> single-machine table above; they are retained so that the earlier reading can be checked against its
> replacement. Two things about that batch are the reason for the re-run, and both are on the record:
> it **mixed two machines**, and one of its runs was **silently degraded under memory pressure** (the
> training path changed without a marker, recorded in this revision's run audit). Its shape control
> also compared **E = 400 with E = 50**, because that batch had no E = 100 point — a mismatch of
> comparison points against the pre-declared `ê/E(400) − ê/E(100)`, and the reason its "failure" and
> the single-machine "pass on the mean" are not in conflict.

> **Result.** The `val`-selected premium is **partly realized** outside the YOLO lineage
> (`prem_test` = **+0.0551 pp** against `prem_val` = **+0.0809 pp**, **68.2 %**, 95 % CI
> **[26.9 %, 123.5 %]**), in the **same direction** as the five arm-cells of §8.6 — and in the same
> direction in **all six runs** (`prem_val > 0` and `prem_test > 0` in 6/6; no run has a negative rate).
> All three `maxDets` conventions give **identical** numbers, confirming (for this corpus) that the
> parameter is not binding here.
>
> **Pre-declared criteria for this escalation** (fixed before the five added seeds were run):
> ① same direction as the original single run; ② a 95 % interval reported; ③ *if that interval contains
> zero*, the wording stays "an anecdote". **① met, ② met, ③ not triggered** — the interval excludes
> zero — so this table is upgraded from an anecdote to an estimate **with** an interval.
>
> **Limits.** **One detector, one corpus**, six seeds — so the result establishes that the
> mechanism is **not confined to the YOLO lineage**, and **not** that non-YOLO pipelines realize ~68 %
> generally. The aggregate's agreement with the single-run value (68.8 %) is a **coincidence of a wide
> per-run spread**: individual rates run from **2.8 % to 174.8 %**, one of them above 100 %. The
> realization rate also has a **small denominator** (mean `prem_val` = +0.0809 pp; the original run's was
> +0.0932 pp), so the ratio is far more
> sensitive to single-run variation than the directional statement `prem_test > 0`. The absolute
> premium is an order of magnitude below the YOLO-side cells of §8.6 (+0.865 … +1.469 pp) and
> **must not be compared with them**: the detector, corpus, dataset scale and absolute mAP level all
> differ. The evaluation-side self-test (`ground truth as predictions` = **0.990099** at
> `maxDets = 1000`**, **0.962854** at 100) is reported in the project log for that pass (2026-09-16). **Why it is not
> exactly 1.0.** Feeding the ground truth back in is not capped by the interpolation grid: measured locally
> (`work/c23_eval_ceiling_20261001.py`, `pycocotools`, gt = predictions, score 1.0), a **one-box self-test reads
> exactly 1.000000** at both `maxDets` settings. The 101-point grid turns a **recall cap** `r` into
> `round(101 r)/101` -- a self-test with 100 detections against 150 ground-truth boxes reads **67/101 =
> 0.663366** (measured) -- so the logged **0.990099 = 100/101** says this pass's maximum recall was
> **100/101 ~ 0.9901**: one ground-truth box went unmatched. It is a **shortfall to investigate, not the
> evaluator's ceiling**; the lower reading at
> `maxDets = 100` is the evaluator's own cap truncating a crowded image's boxes. Neither figure is
> evidence about the detector — it is a check on the evaluation stack, and it is **1.0 up to that
> ceiling**.

### Table S34c — cross-lineage realization test, **second corpus** (SFCHD, non-YOLO detector)

**Table S34c.** *6.5* — the same question as S34, asked of a **second corpus**, so that the cross-lineage
reading rests on direction agreement across two corpora rather than on one. Detector, recipe and evaluation
are held fixed: torchvision `fasterrcnn_resnet50_fpn`, COCO-initialised, 640-px input, batch 8, SGD lr 0.005
with a 1,000-iteration warmup, cosine schedule, 40 epochs, **one run per GPU**, `best` by the `val` curve's
argmax, `test` inferred **once** for `best` and once for `last`. The probe lineage is our own
`run_3way_v2.py` (a seed-carrying variant of the S34 script); the two differ in warmup and in the final-epoch
endpoint, so the two tables are **direction-comparable, not magnitude-comparable** — consistent with the
S34 column being no basis for a cross-detector comparison either.

Corpus: **SFCHD** (two classes, `hat` / `person`). SFCHD is one of the six corpora whose released
configuration gives `val` and `test` the **same directory** (**S1**, row 13) — that is, one of the corpora
this article counts as lacking an independent held-out split. Rather than inherit that, we **carved a
verified-disjoint selection set** from its 20 % training pool (2,413 images) by a **fixed permutation seed**
(`SEED=20261002`, written into the carve script; first 400 images), leaving train 2,013 and keeping the
readout split (6,033) **read-only**; the three sets are pairwise disjoint (asserted in the script). The
corpus was carved **independently on each of two machines** with that same script and seed, and the two
carve manifests are **identical line by line** (md5 `8be82f1f1bfb41a0cd5b4f96cbaa356c`), so the 12 runs are
one corpus, not two. Positive control: with ground truth as predictions, every checkpoint reaches
**0.990099** (ideal 1/1.01) at `maxDets=1000` — the caliper used in S34 — and no reading is written if it
fails. Per-image detections do not reach the `maxDets` cap on this corpus, which is why all three tiers
agree.

| `maxDets` | corpus | n | `prem_val` mean (SD) | `prem_test` mean (SD) | sign, `val` / `test` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 100 | SFCHD | 12 | +0.5216 (0.1544) | +0.1233 (0.1144) | 12/0 · 11/1 |
| **300** | **SFCHD** | **12** | **+0.5216** (0.1544) | **+0.1282** (0.1150) | **12/0 · 11/1** |
| 1000 | SFCHD | 12 | +0.5216 (0.1544) | +0.1233 (0.1144) | 12/0 · 11/1 |
| 300 | VisDrone (S34, for direction only) | 6 | +0.0807 (0.0390) | +0.0383 (0.0868) | 6/0 · 5/1 |

**Pre-declared reading** (fixed before the runs). **C3 — direction agreement — is met**: both corpora have a
positive `prem_val` mean and a positive `prem_test` mean, and each corpus is same-signed in at least five of
its six runs (VisDrone 6/6 and 5/6; SFCHD 12/12 and 11/12). **Wording cap**: this licenses "two-corpus
cross-lineage probe" and nothing stronger; the detector is still one.

**Limits.** Direction only: the corpora differ by an order of magnitude in absolute mAP50-95 (≈8 versus
≈35), so their premiums are not comparable. The VisDrone `prem_test` interval **includes zero**
(**[−0.022, +0.103]**, bootstrap), so the directional verdict rests on the **sign counts** and on SFCHD; the
per-run spread is wide (individual `prem_test` **−0.0853 … +0.3097 pp**), the seeding effect across machines
is absent (`prem_val` correlation **+0.040**, n = 6 shared seeds), and removing any single run leaves every
corpus/arm mean same-signed. Two runs differ between S34 and S34c that are worth naming: S34's six runs are
the original run plus five seeds aggregated at the time, whereas S34c's twelve runs were all run under
`run_3way_v2.py`; the two VisDrone `prem_val` means (+0.0809 and +0.0807) agree to **0.0002 pp**, which we
report as consistency, not as replication.
