## S11 · Formalisation: propositions and proofs (companion to §3.1 of the article)

Notation as in §3.1 of the article: V(e) = M_{S_val}(W(e)), T(e) = M_{S_test}(W(e)), ê = argmax_e V(e),
e_f = the final checkpoint, premium_val = V(ê) − V(e_f), premium_test = T(ê) − T(e_f).

### Proposition 1 (non-identifiability of the raw gap)

**Statement.** V(ê) − T(ê) = [V(e_f) − T(e_f)] + [(V(ê) − V(e_f)) − (T(ê) − T(e_f))]; hence for any
candidate difficulty term δ′ the residual o′ = G − δ′ reproduces the observation exactly, and no
estimator using only the endpoint pair (ê, e_f) identifies δ or o separately.

**Proof.** Substitute V(ê) = V(e_f) + (V(ê) − V(e_f)) and T(ê) = T(e_f) + (T(ê) − T(e_f)); the two
expressions are equal identically. For the second claim, fix any δ′ ∈ ℝ and set o′ = G − δ′; then
δ′ + o′ = G, which is the only equation the data impose. ∎

**Remark (which assumption buys identifiability).** Differencing two arms with matched seeds gives
Δgap = E[G_base − G_strat] = E[(δ_base − δ_strat) + (o_base − o_strat)]. The pairing assumption is
E[δ_base − δ_strat] = 0, which holds when the two arms are trained and evaluated on one corpus and one
split pair. Under an aliasing configuration both arms' δ are maxima of the *same* split's noise, so
the assumption fails by construction: this is the formal content of §12. **What that does and does not say**: it says the equal-difficulty assumption cannot be *assumed* under an aliasing configuration; it does **not** say that any realized contrast is non-zero, nor that aliasing makes a shift automatically detectable — those are statements about measurements, and they are made (or not) where the measurements are.

### Proposition 2 (exact three-component identity)

**Statement.** Δgap = Δδ_f + Δprem_val − Δprem_test, where Δδ_f = (V(e_f) − T(e_f))_base −
(V(e_f) − T(e_f))_strat, Δprem_val = (premium_val)_base − (premium_val)_strat, and Δprem_test is the
same for the realized premiums.

**Proof.** Apply Proposition 1 to each arm and subtract; the split-difficulty terms do not cancel but
combine into Δδ_f, and the bracket terms are Δprem_val − Δprem_test. ∎

**Verification.** The identity holds to the printed digit on all 13 scanned cells (Table S10 prints seven of them; the other six cells' components are in `work/gap_mechanism_20260916.txt`) and on
both escalated cells at n = 10: −1.226 = (−1.583) + (+0.090) + (+0.267) and
−3.315 = (−7.072) + (−0.213) + (+3.971) (`work/x1_aux_20260918.txt`); the three components sum to **−3.314** at full
> precision, so the printed −3.315 differs from the component sum by **0.001 pp** — rounding, not a residual.

### Remark 3 (the sign of Δgap does not identify a mechanism; not a separate result)

**Statement.** If the three components of Proposition 2 are of the same order, then for every target
sign s ∈ {+1, −1} there exist admissible component values whose sum has sign s; hence the observed
sign does not determine which component dominates.

**Proof.** Formalise "of the same order" as **K/2 ≤ |a|, |b|, |c| ≤ K**. Take (a, b, c) = (K, K/2, −K/2), whose sum is +K > 0, and (a, b, c) = (−K, −K/2, K/2), whose sum is −K < 0. In both choices every component lies in [−K, K] and has magnitude in [K/2, K], so both are admissible under the hypothesis and both are of the same order. Since the target sign s was arbitrary, either sign is reachable. ∎

**Empirical support.** Measured medians of the three components are **1.07 / 0.54 / 0.93 pp** (same
order), the 13-cell scan contains **both signs** (ten positive, three negative; −2.46 to +13.66 pp),
and candidate mechanisms M1/M2/M4/M5 are each refuted by sign mismatches (Table S9). **Two
corrections of record.** ① An earlier version of the companion script tried to demonstrate
reachability by resampling *cells*; that inherits the cells' own sign split (98.4 % negative on the
four cells used) and tests nothing. ② A stronger claim — that the sign is *fragile* — is **not
supported**: perturbing one arm-difference term by ±10 % flips the sign in **0 %** of cells
(±50 %: 23 %), with a median cancellation ratio of **0.485**. The proposition is therefore stated as
"the dominant term varies by corpus and the sign is a sum of same-order terms", which is what the
data support (`work/theory_p3a_correct_20260919.txt`).

### Proposition 4 (realization rate = 1 − winner's-curse share)

**Statement.** Assume V(e) = v(e) + ε_e and T(e) = v(e) + τ + η_e with E[ε] = E[η] = 0, ε, η
independent of v, and η independent of the selection event {ê = e}. Then
E[premium_test] = E[premium_val] − WC with WC := E[ε_ê − ε_{e_f}] ≥ 0, so
realization rate := E[premium_test] / E[premium_val] = 1 − WC / E[premium_val] ≤ 1.

**Proof.** (i) E[premium_test] = E[T(ê) − T(e_f)] = E[v(ê) − v(e_f)] + E[η_ê − η_{e_f}]; the second
term is 0 by the stated independence and mean-zero assumption. (ii) E[premium_val] = E[V(ê) − V(e_f)]
= E[v(ê) − v(e_f)] + E[ε_ê − ε_{e_f}]. (iii) Subtracting (i) from (ii) yields
E[premium_test] = E[premium_val] − E[ε_ê − ε_{e_f}]. (iv) Since ê maximises V = v + ε, the selected
checkpoint carries positive noise in expectation, so E[ε_ê − ε_{e_f}] ≥ 0; the equality case is
selection on the signal alone. ∎

**Which assumption carries which half.** The inequality (≤) needs only "ê is chosen using V" — that
is exactly the convention under audit, so the *direction* is robust. The closed form additionally
needs η to stay out of the selection; under aliasing (`val` and `test` the same split) the two noises
coincide and the closed form does not apply — which is why §12 treats the aliased cells' realization
figure as a magnitude statement about bias, not as mechanism evidence.

**Tests on the archive** (`work/theory_p4_test_20260919.py`, `work/theory_meta_and_tests_20260919.py`):

| test | result |
|---|---|
| inequality per run (WC > 0) | **+0.463 pp**, t = 8.34, **p = 1.8×10⁻¹²**; realized premium smaller in **68 / 81** runs (sign test p = 3.8×10⁻¹⁰) |
| rate vs `val` noise (terciles) | **65.6 % → 49.8 % → 13.8 %**; Spearman **ρ = −0.466, p = 1.3×10⁻⁵, n = 80** |
| premium level vs noise (σ√(2 ln E)) | **withdrawn**: slope 0.026 ± 0.222, p = 0.82, n = 101 — the premium's level is set by the curve's shape, not its jitter |

### Proposition 5 (family dependence of Benjamini–Hochberg)

**Statement.** For a fixed multiset of p-values and level q, BH's rejection set is determined by the
step-up condition on i/m·q; consequently the rejection count is not monotone under adding hypotheses:
adding sufficiently small p-values raises the thresholds behind them and can turn a previously
non-rejected hypothesis into a rejected one.

**Proof.** BH rejects H_(1..k) for the largest k with p_(k) ≤ (k/m)q. Since (k/m)q is increasing in k,
inserting new small p-values shifts existing ones to higher ranks k, which relaxes their thresholds;
a hypothesis that failed at rank j can therefore pass at rank j + t. ∎

**Instance in this paper.** Adding the two escalated cells' n = 10 p-values (6.5×10⁻⁹, 1.3×10⁻⁸) moved
`shwd2sf` from "does not survive" to "survives" (0.0053 against a line of 0.01154), and the 13-cell
count went from **0 to 3** (`work/recompute_bh_20260917.py`). Declaring the family is therefore a
precondition for the count to be meaningful, not a presentational choice.

---

### Corollary to Proposition 4 (shape v. noise) — derivation and measurements

**Derivation (exact).** For one arm and one budget E let `V_r(e)` be run r's `val` metric at epoch e,
`premium_r = max_e V_r(e) − V_r(E)`, and `m(e) = mean_r V_r(e)`. Put
`shape(E) = max_e m(e) − m(E)` and `ν_r = premium_r − shape(E)`. Then
`premium_r = shape(E) + ν_r` **by construction**: no assumption about the noise distribution is used.
Two immediate consequences: `shape` is a functional of the mean curve alone and is therefore **not** an
independent check on σ — its uncertainty has to be propagated by resampling runs; and `E[ν_r] ≥ 0`, because replacing each run's curve by the mean curve can only lower the
maximum — this is the `val`-side member of the winner's-curse family of Proposition 4.

**Measurement 1 — the premium's level is shape, and the noise term is flat in E** (same corpus,
pretraining, two arms, five seeds; only the epoch budget changes; run as the **single-machine clean batch** with early stopping disabled — `work/prop6_clean_b_20260925.py`, the same pipeline as `work/prop6_shape_noise_20260920.py` and the generator of `release_shape-noise-by-budget.csv`):

| E | premium (mean of arms) | `shape`/premium |
|---|---|---|
| 50 | +0.444 | 71.2 % |
| 100 | +0.682 | 90.5 % |
| 200 | +0.971 | 87.7 % |
| 400 | +1.272 | 87.6 % |

Two columns are printed rather than three: `shape` is `premium` x (`shape`/premium), so a `shape`
column would only duplicate a product of the two columns beside it (an earlier printing carried it
empty, together with two trailing blank columns, which is what the review round flagged).

**Selection rule behind these four rows, and the `n` of each cell.** The series is the
**single-machine clean batch**: one host (B), early stopping **disabled** (`G3CLEAN_NOSTOP=1`), and each
budget keeps only the runs that **completed epochs 1..E**; two arms (`base`, `lr005`) × seeds 42–46,
so **n = 5 per arm** (8 arm-cells, 40 runs), the merged row weighting the two arms equally after
averaging within an arm. `shape` is the arm's **mean-curve peak minus its final value**, exactly as in
the derivation above. Recomputation and release file: `work/prop6_clean_b_20260925.py` →
`release_shape-noise-by-budget.csv` (the same pipeline as `work/prop6_shape_noise_20260920.py`, the
entry point the reproduction map names, which computed the earlier cross-machine reading).
**The with-early-stopping reading is not deleted**: `+0.907` over all ten E = 400 runs — four of them
stopped early, at effective budgets 388/318/362/375 — remains the reading the article's §3.1 corollary
carries, where it holds the pre-declared verdict, and Table S34b keeps the re-run's per-arm record.
Only this table's premium and share columns move to the clean reading, which is why E = 400 now reads
**+1.272** and **87.6 %** and agrees with that corollary's primary reading (**+1.2717**).

**Four-budget outcome (pre-registered criteria C1–C3).** On the **with-early-stopping** runs the verdict is the pre-declared one and stands: C1 — monotone rise of the premium with E — is **not met**, the series peaking at E = 200 and falling at E = 400 (four of the ten E = 400 runs stopped early under the protocol's own `patience`, at effective budgets 388/318/362/375; on the six full runs the E = 400 mean is +1.278 pp). C2 — the σ√(2 ln E) form — is **not rejected**: premium(400)/premium(100) = 1.329 against the predicted 1.141, inside the pre-declared band [0.86, 1.43]. C3 — the shape control — **passes** (ê/E 0.833 → 0.815). **The single-machine clean re-run (early stopping disabled, all four budgets present) gives a different verdict, and it is the one now reported**: arm-equal means **+0.4439 / +0.6824 / +0.9712 / +1.2717**, so **C1 is met** (monotone non-decreasing; per-run regression on ln E: slope **+0.3999**, se **0.0503**, t = **7.950**, p = **1.325×10⁻⁹**, n = 40); **C2 is out of band** (premium(400)/premium(100) = **1.8636** against the predicted 1.141, band [0.86, 1.43] ⇒ the scaling **stays withdrawn** and the shape reading holds); **C3 passes on the pre-declared mean reading** (ê/E **0.833 → 0.787**, |Δ| = **0.046** ≤ 0.05) while **failing per arm** (lr005 |Δ| = **0.065**; base **0.028**). Table S34b carries the per-arm values, the superseded cross-machine reading and the reason the series was re-run.

E = 50 → 200: `shape` ×2.70, premium ×2.19, `sd(ν)` ×0.92; the σ√(2 ln E) form would predict ×1.24.
The pre-registered decisive test of that form is criterion C2 (band [0.86, 1.43] on
premium(400)/premium(100)); its verdict is reported in Tables S31–S33 and in §3.2.

**Measurement 2 — where the realized gain goes** (40 runs with per-epoch `test` readouts on a 5-epoch
grid, `val` from each run's own `results.csv`; selection on `val` only, readout on `test` only):

| quantity | value |
|---|---|
| premium(`val`) | **+0.981 pp** |
| premium(`test`), oracle peak on `test` | +0.704 pp (**71.7 %** of the `val` premium) |
| premium(`test`), using the `val` argmax | **+0.211 pp (21.5 %)** |
| runs whose `val` and `test` peaks agree | **17.5 %** |
| median \|ê_val − ê_test\| | 10 epochs (grid step 5) |

So the low realization rate is a **peak-alignment** phenomenon, not an absence of gain: an oracle that
could pick the `test` peak would realize 71.7 % of the `val` premium, and `val`-selection retains 30 %
of that.

**Measurement 3 — selection rules** (same 40 runs; every rule chooses on `val`, readout on `test`):

| rule | realized `test` premium | captured share | cost to the reported number | within one step of the `test` peak |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| argmax (current convention) | +0.211 | **30.0 %** | 0.000 | 45.0 % |
| mean of top-2 `val` epochs | +0.406 | 57.7 % | 0.690 | 55.0 % |
| mean of top-3 `val` epochs | +0.380 | 54.0 % | 0.596 | 72.5 % |
| mean of top-5 `val` epochs | +0.490 | **69.6 %** | 0.727 | 62.5 % |
| argmax of a 5-epoch moving average | +0.459 | 65.2 % | 0.526 | 70.0 % |
| final epoch (no selection) | +0.000 | 0.0 % | 0.981 | 2.5 % |

**What these measurements are not.** They are a **post-hoc comparison on the same runs**, not a
pre-registered experiment: the rules were evaluated after the runs existed. The grid is 5 epochs wide,
so "within one step" means ±5 epochs; all choices are made on `val` and every readout is on the other
split; and the oracle row is an upper bound, not an attainable protocol.

**A candidate mechanism for the plateau rule — stated as a candidate, not as a result.** The natural
explanation of Measurement 3 is that the argmax of a noisy curve is pulled towards epochs whose noise
happens to be high, so its underlying value sits below the true peak (the classical peak-shift penalty,
of order σ²/|v″|), while a top-k average estimates the peak *region* with lower variance. That story
makes a sharp prediction: the plateau advantage should **grow with the noise of the `val` curve**. We
tested it, and it is **not established**:

* noise estimator: `σ̂_seed` = mean over epochs of the cross-seed standard deviation at that epoch
  (`V_r(e) − mean_r V_r(e)`), i.e. the run-to-run dispersion the ten-seed design is about — not the
  residual around a smoothing of one run's own curve (which mixes curvature into "noise" and, with that
  estimator, gives ρ = +0.04, p = 0.79);
* per run (n = 40): ρ(`σ̂_seed`, plateau − argmax) = **+0.309, p = 0.052**;
* per cell (n = **4** cells, the honest counting unit): ρ = **+0.800**, slope **+0.406 ± 0.101** per pp of
  `σ̂_seed`, r = **+0.943**, but **p = 0.057**;
* the four cells read: `σ̂_seed` 0.629 → +0.194 pp, 0.639 → +0.154 pp, 1.125 → +0.318 pp, 1.201 → +0.447 pp —
  i.e. both noisy cells show about twice the advantage of both quiet cells, with no exceptions.

**What this is and is not.** The *advantage* of the plateau rule is established (Measurement 3, and the
paired test +0.278 pp, t = 3.678, p = 7.1×10⁻⁴, positive in 26 of 40 runs). The *mechanism* is
**consistent but not significant on four cells**, so we report it as a candidate and not as a result.
Settling it needs more corpus cells with per-epoch `test` readouts — the direction of the effect is
already pinned down by the four we have, but four points cannot carry a significance claim. The
`test` side of the remaining corpora has since been closed (Table S38), so the extension can be run
on the release; we do not read it here rather than report it as settled.

### 13.2 §3.2: the per-comparison reasoning, moved from the article

Where the bias does move, it moves with what the selection sees: the split-size comparison held the test
side fixed and varied only the size (both arms moving the same way), and the epoch-budget comparison left
the peak’s relative position essentially unchanged, so neither is a shape artefact. The third-corpus
replication evaluated its test side separately and points the same way as the 81 runs of §8.6 and the 40
of §5.1.

Sources: `work/g1p_verdict_20260919.py`, `work/g2_and_g1p_verdict_20260919.txt`,
`work/g1pp_g3_g4_verdicts_20260919.txt`, `work/theory_p4_test_20260919.py`.

### 8.7b The per-epoch sign detail, moved from the article

`shwd2sf`'s Δgap is positive at **all 19 checkpoints**; `smoke2sf`'s is **negative at the first
three — epochs 5, 10 and 15 — and positive at all 16 checkpoints from epoch 20 onward**; the
four-epoch grid began at epoch 20, so it could not have seen this. At epochs 60 and 80 the val-side
arm difference is −0.176 / −0.384 and the test-side one −1.051 / −1.445 — **reproducing the published
values to the printed digit** — while Δgap stays positive: **the arm ordering flips on both splits at
once, their difference does not.**

### 13.2b The four-budget detail, moved from the article

Four of the ten E = 400 runs stopped early under the protocol's own `patience` (effective budgets
388/318/362/375); on the six that ran to 400 the E = 400 mean is +1.278 pp — and that one restriction
flips both criteria: on the six full runs the series is monotone (C1 met) and
premium(400)/premium(100) = 1.873 falls *outside* the band (C2 rejected, the shape reading restored).

### 5.4 The two external critiques, in full (moved from the article)

The correction the critique forced: the paired-difference form cancels the difficulty term under the
pairing assumption stated in §3.1, so the quantity we report is identifiable where the raw
`M_val − M_test` is not.

### 13.3 The peak-agreement predictor (companion to §3.2 of the article)

**Setup.** The released per-run file carries, for each of the 40 paired runs, the validation peak
(`e_val_peak`), the test peak (`e_test_peak`) and the two premiums. Both peaks are taken on the
**same 20-point checkpoint grid** `(0, 5, 10, …, 95)`; that is the convention under which
`same_epoch` is meaningful, and it is verified here: recomputing both peaks on that grid reproduces
the released `e_val_peak` **40/40**, `e_test_peak` **40/40** and `same_epoch` **40/40**.

**Per-run values for the group where the peaks agree** (the other 33 runs' values are the released
columns themselves):

| run | `e_val_peak` | `e_test_peak` | Δgap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `r10_shwd2sf_base100_3way_s49n` | 75 | 75 | **+0.0561** |
| `r10_shwd2sf_lr005_100ep_3way_s45n` | 75 | 75 | **+0.4099** |
| `r10_smoke2sf_base100_3way_s42n` | 80 | 80 | **−0.2144** |
| `r10_smoke2sf_base100_3way_s46n` | 85 | 85 | **−0.5838** |
| `r10_smoke2sf_base100_3way_s47n` | 65 | 65 | **−0.2019** |
| `r10_smoke2sf_base100_3way_s49n` | 75 | 75 | **−0.2174** |
| `r10_smoke2sf_lr005_100ep_3way_s43n` | 85 | 85 | **+1.6649** |

**Group summaries.** Peaks agree (n = 7): mean Δgap **+0.1305 pp**, median **−0.2019 pp**, four of
seven non-positive. Peaks differ (n = 33): mean **+0.9056 pp**, median **+0.9537 pp**, four of
thirty-three non-positive. Across all 40 runs Δgap spans **−0.7724 to +2.0454 pp**.

**Permutation test.** Reassigning the agreement label at random across the 40 runs 20 000 times, the
absolute difference of the two group means is at least the observed **|0.7751| pp** in **0.0091** of
draws (seed 42, `random.shuffle`); the agreement indicator's AUC for Δgap > 0 is **0.2969**, with a
two-sided permutation p of **0.0196**.

**The same relabelling on the median, which is the estimator these groups actually support.** The
agreement groups are separated far more clearly by their medians than by their means. Observed
|median(agree) − median(differ)| = |**−0.2019** − **+0.9537**| = **1.1556 pp**; 20 000 relabellings
of the 40 Δgap values (seed 42, same convention) reach that or more in **0.0011** of draws (seeds 7 and
2026 give 0.0009 and 0.0014; the lower-median definition gives 0.0006). **The group means are the
weaker summary, and the mean of the agreement group is carried by one run**: the agreement group's
mean is **+0.1305 pp** on a group sum of **+0.9134 pp**, of which the single run
`r10_smoke2sf_lr005_100ep_3way_s43n` contributes **+1.6649 pp**; dropping it moves the group mean to
**−0.1252 pp** while leaving the median at **−0.2019 pp**, and the median-difference permutation p
becomes **0.0007**. **The reading this licences is a lower median Δgap under agreement — not "the
premium is realized almost in full"**: four of the seven agreement runs are non-positive, and the
summary that survives the single-run sensitivity is the median. Script (read-only, no retraining):
`work/r98_median_perm_20260927.py`, over `release_selection-transfer_perseed.csv` and
`xeval_perepoch_20260918/matrix_perepoch.csv`.

**What this table is not.** The agreement group is **not** a group in which the premium is realized:
its **median Δgap is negative** and four of its seven runs are non-positive, so agreement is a
condition under which the *mean* deviation is small, not a guarantee that the selected checkpoint
carries over. The result is also a **partial** predictor: it conditions on one observable and leaves
the sign's variation inside each group unexplained, it rests on **7 runs** in the agreement group,
and it is defined **only on the 5-epoch grid used here**, which was not refined.

Sources: `work/partial_predictor_final_20260922.py` (grouping, permutation test, AUC),
`work/probe_evalpeak_convention_20260922.py` (the grid convention and its 40/40 reproduction); the
grid and premium columns are the released `matrix_perepoch.csv` and
`release_selection-transfer_perseed.csv`.

## S12 · Method detail moved from the article

This appendix holds the measurement, protocol and record detail whose **conclusions stay in the article**. It is moved rather than cut: a reader who wants to check a claim reads the same measurements here, while the article keeps one sentence at each site. Identifiers that describe one machine's internal bookkeeping have been replaced by descriptors; the measurements themselves are unchanged.

### 3.1 Formalisation — the statements displaced from the article

§3.1 of the article now keeps each statement in compressed form, the adoption declaration, the two testable
consequences of the adopted identity, and the corollary; the full statements with their derivations and
proofs are in §S11. What the compression displaced is reproduced here **verbatim**, so that no number and
no qualification leaves the paper — including the attribution sentence that separates what the
generalization-gap literature establishes from what this paper states as its own formalisation, and the
disclosure that the sign of Δgap is not claimed to be *fragile*.

**Definition (the raw gap is not identifiable from the pair alone).** V(ê) − T(ê) = [V(e_f) − T(e_f)] +
[(V(ê) − V(e_f)) − (T(ê) − T(e_f))], i.e. **G = δ + o** with δ a split-difficulty term and o a
selection-optimism term. **The additive decomposition, and the qualitative reading that a single-model endpoint pair cannot
separate its two terms, are established in the generalization-gap literature [17]**; its **strong
form** — that *any* δ′ fits the observation equally well, so that no endpoint-pair estimator
separates them [19] — is **stated here as a formalisation rather than attributed to that
literature**. **The pairing assumption is what makes Δgap meaningful**:
with matched seeds and one split pair, E[δ_base − δ_strat] = 0, and only then does the arm difference
identify the difference in selection optimism. Under an aliasing configuration that assumption
*fails by construction* — the two arms' δ are two maxima on the same split — which is why §9 cannot
be read as a clean-protocol result.

**Adopted identity, not a claim of this paper (the realization rate is one minus the winner's-curse share).** With V(e) = v(e) + ε_e
and T(e) = v(e) + τ + η_e, **E[premium_test] = E[premium_val] − WC** where **WC := E[ε_ê − ε_e_f] ≥ 0**,
so the realization rate is **1 − WC / E[premium_val] ≤ 1**, with equality only if selection is driven
by signal alone [13] — Smith & Winkler's optimizer's curse written in this paper's notation; **we adopt it as the frame for §8.6–§8.7 and claim no part of it**. **What the per-run check can and cannot settle** (moved from the article): the check reads the **observed** `prem_val − prem_test`, so it corroborates that *consequence* of the inequality; it does **not** settle the latent `E[ε_ê − ε_e_f] ≥ 0`, which no archive decides without the identifying assumption. The inequality needs only that ê is chosen on `val` (the convention under audit); the identity
additionally needs the `test` noise not to enter the selection. **Two consequences, both tested on the archive** — the per-run inequality and the monotonicity in the `val` noise; per-run values, terciles and rank correlation: **Supplementary S11**. This is why §8.6's realization rate and §8.7's per-cell variation are the
same statement at different noise levels, not a contradiction. A third form — that the premium scales as σ√(2 ln E) — was **tested and withdrawn**; the regression and its diagnostic are in **Supplementary S11**. Contemporaneous work measures **≈5×** on the same σ√(2 ln K) bound [18].

**Proposition 2 (exact three-component identity).** Differencing the definition across arms gives
**Δgap = Δδ_f + Δprem_val − Δprem_test** — an identity, not a fit, holding value by value on all 13
scanned cells and both escalated cells at n = 10 (§5.6).

**Remark (the sign of Δgap does not identify a mechanism; not a separate result).** Three measured facts carry this:
the components are of the same order (medians **1.07 / 0.54 / 0.93 pp**), so which dominates
differs by corpus; **both signs occur** in the scanned cells (per-cell table: **Table S6**); every candidate mechanism we could
state is refuted by a
**sign mismatch** (M1/M2/M4/M5, Table S9). **What we do not claim** is that the sign is *fragile*: a perturbation analysis (numbers in **Supplementary S11**) leaves it stable under a ±10 % change of a single arm-difference term, so the honest statement is "the dominant term varies by corpus and the sign is a sum of same-order terms", not "the sign is noise".

**Proposition 5 (Benjamini–Hochberg depends on the declared family).** The step-up thresholds are
i/m·q, so adding hypotheses with smaller p-values **raises** the thresholds behind them and the
rejection count is not monotone in the family (proof: **Supplementary S11**). We are ourselves an
instance: escalating two cells to n = 10 moved the 13-cell count from **0 to 3** surviving cells
(§5.1) [20] — so declaring the counting unit and the family is a condition for the number to
mean anything.

**§3.2 as worded in the previous revision** — the per-experiment criteria and their values, replaced in
the article by the verdict list plus the Table S31–S33 pointer. Kept here verbatim for the same reason:


The propositions above are testable, so we ran them on rented GPUs with the **criterion fixed before the
runs**. Six experiments were run; **five of them had their pre-declared criteria judged** — G1 is **void** (its design was diagnosed as confounded), so no verdict is entered for it. **Two of the five judged outcomes are negative** (G1′ and G2) and one design had to be redone — reported as they came out.

The six experiments, their **pre-declared criteria** and how each came out are tabulated in
**Tables S31–S33**: G1 **void** (the two splits differ by 14.7 pp in difficulty on the same
checkpoints, so its −0.582 pp mixes the effect with a level effect); G1″ **met**; G1′ **not met**
(WC = **−0.069 pp**, t = −0.23, p = 0.83, n = 10 — no inflation detectable); G2 **direction met,
magnitude not met**; G3 **met** (and, in the clean re-run without early stopping, **C1 flips to
met while the shape control fails** — S34); G4 **met** (WC = **+5.21 pp**, CI [+4.82, +5.66],
20/20 runs positive). Where the outcome is negative it is reported as negative.

The criteria that were met and those that were not carry one message: **the selection bias moves with
the absolute amount of selection noise and with the number of selection opportunities, but not with
whether the two splits are the same file.** Two consequences do not follow from the numbers alone: the
configuration-level fact of §4 stands — the reported number *is* the selected number — while the
stronger reading, that aliasing *adds* a measurable amount on top of it, does not. That identity's WC
is best read as the winner's-curse share of the selected gain, a quantity set by the noise. **That reading yields a partial predictor of Δgap's sign, which we test here.** On the shared 5-epoch checkpoint grid the `val` and `test` peaks agree in only **17.5 %** of runs, and conditioning on that single observable splits the 40 runs cleanly: where the peaks agree the `val`-selected premium is realized almost in full (mean **+0.13 pp**, n = 7), and where they differ it is **systematically not** (mean **+0.91 pp**, n = 33; permutation p = **0.009**). **This is a partial predictor, not an explanation of the sign, and its limits are part of it:** the agree-group contains four of seven runs whose gap is non-positive, so agreement is not a guarantee of realization; n is small; and the split is defined on the 5-epoch grid, which we did not refine. The per-run table and the permutation test are in **Supplementary S11**. The
per-comparison reasoning and the sources are in **Tables S31–S33**.

+1.77 / +1.46 / +1.54 — **again larger for the baseline arm**, as in §5.1
Tier 1).

### 9.3 The seed convention: how the ten seeds are realized

Per-run randomness is installed as a **deterministic permutation of the training file order**
(`--shuffle-seed`, set to the number in the run's own name), not through the framework's `seed`
argument: ultralytics 8.4.120's data pipeline does not draw from `args.seed`, and on two of these
corpora different `--seed` values produced **identical** epoch-by-epoch replays.

**The same convention and the same check hold for the cells escalated in §5.1**: all twenty
`args.yaml` of the `dota15` cell record `seed: 42` while its ten per-seed premiums are ten distinct
values, and the forty runs of the two escalated Tier-2 cells likewise give ten distinct values in
each arm-cell. Script: `work/check_seed_convention_20260918.py`.

### 5.4 Two external critiques, checked (one refuted, one judged non-identifiable)
Full report: `the two-critique verification record`; script
`analysis/work/verify_two_critiques_20260916.py` → `eval_validity/verify_two_critiques_20260916.txt`.

**(I) "The premium difference may be noise-level" — refuted.** The critique compared it against **0.515 pp**,
the **single-run** data-order component of §8.4, and concluded "underpowered, not significant". The
quantity under test is a **difference of arm means**, with uncertainty the SE of that difference
(**0.058–0.120 pp**); the t and p values of §5.1 Tier 1, and their four CIs excluding zero, settle it.
**The wrong denominator was used; the data are sufficient.**

**(II) "You should report `M_val(ê) − M_test(ê)` directly" — judged non-identifiable.** That quantity mixes
the two splits' difficulty with selection optimism and is not identifiable here; we report the **paired
difference Δgap** instead and **state honestly that its sign is not uniform** (§5.1 Tier 2). **A hard fact that came with it:** in
shwd2sf the arm ordering on `val` is the reverse of that on `test` — see §5.1.

**What these two checks changed.** We used to write "the problem **understates** rather than manufactures a
difference between arms"; (II) corrects this to "**it systematically shifts arm-to-arm comparison, and the
direction varies by corpus**" — the abstract, §2.1, §2.2 and §5.1 rewritten accordingly.

### 5.5 The same component in third-party public logs
Sections 5.1–5.4 use **our own** 569 runs; this asks whether the component **exists in other people's
published logs**.

**Sample.** 24 per-epoch training logs; the **20
complete logs (≥ 40 epochs), from 6 source repositories**, are analysed. The definition is identical to ours:
`premium = best-epoch mAP50-95 − final-epoch mAP50-95`. Ultralytics-style logs report the
**best-validation checkpoint**, so this difference is **an upper bound for** the component contributed by selecting on the
reported split.

> **Table S7** → Supplementary §S8.


**What matters most.** The largest premium (**+5.65 pp**) occurs in an **ordinary published log** (a 47-epoch
segmentation run; final epoch 0.6521, best epoch 42 at 0.7086). Beside this paper's own magnitudes —
**+1.434 pp** (the companion paper's reading, §8.1) and a published controlled ablation of **0.040 mAP50-95**
(§2.1) — **the premium's long tail is larger than both**, its median of the same order as the former.
(A magnitude comparison, **not** a same-unit subtraction: a within-run selection difference versus a
between-arm effect.)

**What it can and cannot support.** **Can**: the component exists in **other people's** published logs, with a
per-log magnitude (all 20 → Supplementary S5).
**Cannot**: a **field-level** distribution — 20 logs from 6 sources, most not stating dataset or protocol.

**The sample is a convenience sample**, not a frame: logs came from public dataset hosts and
one code repository, filtered only by the search terms stated above; no platform-side query is named
because none was systematic. **Any quotation of these magnitudes** (§2.2, §5.5) carries the four
limitations listed below.

**Verifiability (upgraded to "byte-checkable *and* traceable").** Each log's **SHA-256
and byte count** are in `published_logs/SHA256SUMS.txt` (**author-side directory, not part of the released package**); at source level, **24/24 files** matched to a
source path by **content hash or unique byte count** — 8 Kaggle datasets plus 1 GitHub repository
pinned at commit `2417aff0457c`, with **0 untraceable entries** and **24/24 agreement** with
`SHA256SUMS.txt`.

**Four limitations:**

1. **It cannot establish aliasing.** Public logs carry **no YAML**, so we cannot tell whether that run's `val`
   and `test` share a directory. **"The premium exists" and "the benchmark aliases" are two independent
   pieces of evidence and cannot substitute for one another** — this section supports only the former (§4, §6 the latter).
2. **The exact downloaded version is unprovable.** What is provable is "**the bytes we analysed equal these
   hashes**", not "these bytes equal the Kaggle version at the time" (the source table records
   `currentVersionNumber` *at probe time*: `ikram0703` 2, the rest 1).
3. **The endpoint reading is an upper bound**: repositories rarely record which epoch was reported, so we
   assume it was the best.
4. **Licensing**: **5 sources do not state a licence** (Kaggle `Unknown`), and the GitHub source's `LICENSE`
   was not retained. We **do not redistribute** these CSVs, only hashes and source pointers; derived
   statistics should be cited with that caveat.

**A defect we must record (one silent exclusion).** The first script version identified metric columns by the
substring `'mAP50-95' in column`, recognising **only the new Ultralytics spelling**, so the YOLOv5/v3
spelling `metrics/mAP_0.5:0.95` used by `ayyappakorla-yolov3-results` made `read_log` return `None` and the
file **disappeared from the sample with no diagnostic anywhere**. It is a
**200-epoch** complete log (final epoch 0.9011, best epoch 198 at 0.9041, premium **+0.31 pp**). After the fix
the sample went **19 → 20 logs**, **5 → 6 sources**, the median **+1.18 → +1.04 pp**, and positives
**17/19 → 18/20**.

**Three conventions adopted from that failure** (project-wide): ① annotate per row **which column
spelling** the log uses; ② a "**logs that could not be parsed must be listed**" section — **a
guard may exclude, but it may not exclude silently**; ③ read the best epoch from each log's **own epoch
column**, not the row index (most start at 1, the `ayyappakorla` one at 0).

**The four logs not analysed** run for 30, 20, 5 and 1 epoch; the
5-epoch one's "best − mean of last five" gives a meaningless +12.94 pp — **which is why a minimum length must
be imposed**, and we report only `max − final`, for 20 logs.

> **Relation to §5.1.** This section supplies the existence and magnitude of the same component in **other
> people's** data. It **does not enter** §5.1's two-tier conclusion (whose evidence is our own paired
> design), and the two bodies of evidence do **not** corroborate one another.

### 6.1 Which reported number the COCO row judges, and this table's first-version correction
> **Table S11** → Supplementary §S8.

**Which number the `reported` unit judges.** For one row — COCO — it depends which reported number is
meant: community tables report the **server-scored `test-dev`** value, which no local run can be selected
on (we count it independent), while a paper's own ablations report its **`val`** value, which is also the
selection split. First reading: `reported` **12/19**, clean **3/19**; second: **13/19** and **2/19**. No
other row is affected; the abstract prints the first.

**Correction to the first version of this table.** It **put 2/19 under
`yolo_dist`**, contradicting the recomputation: `yolo_dist` is **4/19** (PASCAL VOC and SFCHD's **literal
two-key** alias, SHWD's `train == val`, Mendeley's generator alias), **2/19 being only the subset** the audit
reported. The table makes **a unit a whole column**, ordered as §3's four definitions.


### 6.2 "Same directory" has four levels, and the audit reported only the narrowest
> **Table S12** → Supplementary §S8.


L1 is what the audit enumerated; L3 carries most of the *reported* layer's non-independence.

### 6.3 We do not reproduce the original audit's two numbers
> **Table S13** → Supplementary §S8.

### 6.5 Across frameworks: is the convention specific to YOLO?
Is "the reported metric comes from a non-independent split" a YOLO writing problem, or the default of the
field? §6.1–§6.3 rest on the **generic YOLO distribution package**.

We retrieved, at **pinned commits**, the official configurations and training scripts of **five pinned repositories — 25 files**,
covering six frameworks in total, since the Ultralytics distribution package is the `yolo_dist`
unit of §6.1 and is supplied separately (SHA-256 and byte counts per file in `_xframe/SHA256SUMS.txt`; pins in
`PINNED_COMMITS.txt`; script `work/analyze_xframe_20260916.py` → `xframe_evidence_20260916.txt`), then
read mechanically which split each framework **reports** on and whether that split is **also** used for
checkpoint selection.

> **Table S14** → Supplementary §S8.


**Two conclusions: one widens the problem, one narrows our own claim.**

**(C-1, widening) Reporting on `val` is the shared default of all six frameworks, because of
annotation availability.** COCO's test annotations are not public — Detectron2 writes the test split's
annotation file as `image_info_test2017.json` (**image information only**), PaddleDetection reads the
**same val annotation file** through a ground-truth-free `ImageFolder`. This is why the reported-layer count is what it is: where annotations are not public, the default
path is to report on whichever split can be scored locally, and that split is usually also the
selection split. **COCO sits on the boundary** — its `test-dev` is server-scored and cannot be
selected on, so we count its community-reported number as independent, while a paper's own
ablation numbers are not; §6.1 states both readings and both counts. **On benchmarks like COCO the
coincidence is not an author's oversight — annotation availability pushes everyone onto the same
split**, the default path itself.

**(C-2, narrowing) But "select the checkpoint on that split" is not the shared default — only the YOLO
lineage does it.** Ultralytics (`best.pt`) and YOLOX (`best_ap`) select by the validation metric;
Detectron2 (`EVAL_PERIOD = 0`), MMDetection (no `save_best`), DETR (no best tracking) and
PaddleDetection (none found) **report the final epoch**. §5's selection-premium claim therefore reads:
**"within the pipeline family that selects by the validation metric by default (the YOLO lineage), the
convention systematically shifts arm-to-arm comparison"** — **not** "the default of the field". The
abstract and the §2.1 table claim about YOLO distributions only, consistent with this.

**Four limitations of this section** (so it is not read as a census): ① we read **default
configurations**, which users may change; ② only **5 pinned repositories / 25 official files** — **fixed-point sampling,
not a census**; ③ the pins are **default-branch heads** and will move, hence the SHA-256 and commit per
file; ④ as in §4 and §6, a **configuration-level audit — no dataset was downloaded**.

---

### 8.1 The protocol
Split the pool three ways — a training subset, a **carved validation** subset for selection, a **test**
subset for reporting; carved and test are disjoint, so the reported number is no
longer the maximum a selection rule saw. Three requirements make it trustworthy:
**carve out of the pool** (the test split was never in training), **verify all three intersections are
zero** (by filename), and **freeze the endpoint definition in writing** — which checkpoint counts as "the"
reported number, whose omission is defect 3 in §10 and the source of the conflict in §7.3.

### 8.3 A direct measurement of selection inflation
Extending the same cell from n = 6 to n = 9, the mean falls **systematically**:

> **Table S18** → Supplementary §S8.


**The magnitude is 9 %–29 %, taken against each cell's own n = 6 mean rather than the pooled headline effect.** **The estimand is the change
in the cell mean when the seed count rises from 6 to 9** (two nested counts of the same runs; no new
training), so this measures **seed inflation**; reading it as "how much was selection
rather than signal" is an interpretation we do not test here.

### 8.4 The three variance components: the excluded one is the smaller in this sample
> **Table S19** → Supplementary §S8.


**Reading (favourable to this paper, at three initializations):** on the same unit, **initialization
(0.146 pp) fluctuated less than data order (0.515 pp)** when this was written — **the component we excluded
was not the dominant one** — so "the reported power is an optimistic upper bound" tightened to "the
excluded initialization component is smaller than the dominant seed component". **The five-initialization
extension (2026-09-30) removes that reading**: the gain SD becomes **0.439 pp** with a bootstrap interval
**[0.058, 0.580]** that overlaps the data-order component, so the ranking is retained only as a **sample
statement at n = 3**, not as an established ordering.

**Two limitations must be stated with it**: ① **three** initializations stood behind the original reading
and **five** behind the 2026-09-30 extension, so the SD estimate stays uncertain (the 95 % interval for σ
is roughly [0.5σ̂, 6σ̂] at 2 degrees of freedom and [0.6σ̂, 2.9σ̂] at 4); the extension's interval overlaps
the data-order component, so we write "consistent with", **not** "established" — and the same now applies
to the **ranking** itself. ② That experiment ran on machine A with A-side third-party YAMLs,
the n = 10 cells on machine B under the same protocol; cross-machine comparability must be noted.

### 8.5 Why "same-pipeline pairing" is a necessity, not a habit
Three initializations move the **absolute endpoint** of one configuration by 43.250–43.630 (range
0.38 pp, SD 0.209 pp), comparable to seed noise; **five** (the 2026-09-30 extension) move it by
**43.070–43.630** (range **0.56 pp**, SD **0.253 pp**). **Absolute endpoints are not comparable across source
endpoints; only paired differences within one source endpoint are stable.**

### 5.6 Why Δgap's sign varies by corpus — measurement detail
§5.1 Tier 2 reports the **fact**; with the archived quantities
**the mechanism is not identifiable**, though three candidates are **refuted** and two unsupported.

**Data**: 13 three-way corpus cells × 2 arms (paired seeds n = 3 or 10). Script
`work/gap_mechanism_20260916.py` → `gap_mechanism_20260916.txt`; the data path matches §5.4's and
every cell's Δgap agrees **value by value** with the §5.1 Tier 2 table.

> **Tables S8–S10** → Supplementary §S8.


**First, an identity**: Δgap = (arm difference on val) − (arm difference on test), hence
**Δgap > 0 ⟺ the relative arm difference on `val` is smaller than on `test`**. It compares the two splits
only and **points to no generating cause**.


**An exact decomposition** (an identity explaining the unstable sign — **not** a mechanism).
Because `M_val(ê) = V_final + premium_val`, Δgap expands into three components, **all computable from the
archive**:

> Δgap = (arm difference at the **final** epoch on val) + (**val-side selection gain** difference) − (test-side arm difference)


This is an **identity, not a fit**: for all 13 cells the "check" column (the sum of the three components)
**equals** Δgap. The three components' median magnitudes are **1.07 / 0.54 / 0.93 pp — the same order**, so
**any one of them can flip Δgap's sign**, consistent with §5.1's instability.

**The most informative cell is `mask20`**: the baseline leads the strategy by **+15.78 pp** at the **final
epoch on val**, but the strategy's **val-side selection gain** is **13.10 pp** larger; the two nearly
cancel, leaving Δgap **+1.75** (p = 0.66, not significant). **This cell shows that selection alone
can absorb an arm difference of order 13 pp** — it supports "the convention changes arm-to-arm comparison"
(§5.1 Tier 1) while showing that Δgap's sign **cannot** be used to infer any single cause.

**What this section claims.** Δgap's sign **varies by corpus** is firm (§5.1). Its generating **mechanism is
identifiable in this archive only in part**: separating "the two splits' difficulty" from "selection optimism
(one part on val, one on test)" requires **test metrics at non-selected epochs**, and the archive stores only
the test value of the **selected** checkpoint — supplying it means re-running the test evaluation (see §8.6 and §10, defect 5, for what that closed).
**What remains is bounded and stated, not left as "mechanism to be explained".**

---

### 7.2 The two clean-protocol cells clear significance by n = 6–8 — measurement detail
The clean-protocol seed extension (seeds 42–51) reaches **n = 10**:

> **Table S15** → Supplementary §S8.


> **First-report attribution (a convention we hold to).** The two Δ values here (**+1.434 / +0.507 pp**),
> the three-way remediation protocol itself, and the pre-registered multi-target replication (§9) belong
> to the companion paper and are **cited, not claimed**: both modules were transferred to it by agreement
> (2026-09-21) and are its first report there. What this paper reports first is **the selection premium
> (§5), the 2 × 2 realization of that premium on a disjoint split (§8.6–§8.7), and the formalisation of
> the realization rate (§3.1)**. The companion manuscript is named, and its relation to this paper stated, in the cover
> letter; it carries no identifier while under review, and is available to the editor and the
> reviewers on request. A formal citation follows once both papers are accepted.

**Seed extension is therefore not optional**: it upgrades the conclusion from "significant under the
parametric test only" to "significant under both".

### 7.1 The smallest attainable permutation p at each n — moved from the article

**Why the floor is 2/2ⁿ, and what each n licenses.** For a sign-flip permutation test on n paired
differences the smallest attainable two-sided p is **2/2ⁿ**, because the observed sign pattern is the most
extreme of the 2ⁿ equally likely relabellings; at n = 3 that is **0.25**, so such a cell has **no
resolution at all** and may be read only as "significant under the paired t-test; the non-parametric test
is unavailable at this n". The floors at the n used in this paper are: n = 3 **0.25**; n = 6 **0.031**;
n = 7 **0.0156**; n = 10 **0.0020** — the last being the same quantity as **2/2¹⁰ = 0.001953**, which is
how the tables print it, so the two spellings denote one number and not two. **After §5.1's escalations no
headline cell (Tables 1–2) is n = 3.** The article keeps the general form, the n = 3 case and the fact
that no headline cell is n = 3, and points here for the derivation and the remaining floors.

**The power note for the n = 3 cells, with its derivation (moved from the article).** Recovering each Tier-2 cell's sd from its own printed 95 % CI as `sd = half-width ÷ (t_{9,0.975}/√10)` gives **0.20–0.67 pp** across the four escalated cells; a paired comparison at n = 3 then needs `(t_{2,0.975} + t_{2,0.80})·sd/√3 = 3.10·sd`, and the **exact 80 %-power MDE from the non-central t (df = 2, two-sided α = 0.05) is `3.26·sd`** — i.e. **0.65–2.19 pp** for 80 % power at a two-sided α = 0.05, **at or above every Tier-1 effect the article reports** (+0.27 to +0.53 pp); at n = 10 the same derivation gives an MDE of `0.99·sd`, i.e. **0.2–0.7 pp**, the scale at which the four escalated cells are judged. The nine cells left at n = 3 cannot carry an inference at this design and are reported descriptively only.

### 7.3 A cross-archive endpoint conflict on the same run (investigated — and itself a disclosure) — measurement detail
One run, `t2_mask2mende_base100_s42n`, is recorded with **two different checkpoints**: one row reports
the **final** epoch (67.12), the other the **best** (70.06).

> **Table S16** → Supplementary §S8.


**We follow B** and report both. Not an error in either archive: evidence that **the project had no
written convention for which endpoint constitutes "the" reported number** — §10's defect 3, the reason §3
insists that a count declare its unit.

---

---

### 5.1 Direction: the two tiers — measurement detail (moved from the article)

**The pairing arithmetic, in full.** The tested quantity is the paired difference
d(seed) = premium_baseline(seed) − premium_strategy(seed), within cell and seed. The right scale is the
SE of the difference (**0.058–0.120 pp**), and the four paired differences are
**+0.416 / +0.526 / +0.272 / +0.449 pp**, with p from 7.6×10⁻⁶ to 1.1×10⁻³ and all four 95 % CIs
excluding zero (CI bounds **[+0.255, +0.578]**, **[+0.386, +0.665]**, **[+0.141, +0.403]**,
**[+0.309, +0.589]**; the unpaired Welch t is **5.68**).

**Metric sensitivity, in full.** Re-running the estimator on `metrics/mAP50(B)` keeps the sign in all
four cells but moves one cell's paired difference into the noise: smoke2sf falls from
**+0.416 pp (p = 1.9×10⁻⁴)** to **+0.063 pp (p = 0.54)**, while shwd2sf **+0.847**, a2d15 **+0.406**
(p = **0.0019**) stay significant. Script `work/t9_ap50_sensitivity_20260924.py`.

**The `dota15` escalation.** It entered at n = 3 with a parametric p = **0.039**, below the resolution of
any sign-flip test there; the pre-declared consequence was that a ten-seed paired difference failing to
be positive with ≥ 8/10 same-sign and a CI excluding zero would remove the cell. Ten seeds (42–51) gave
**+0.449 pp**, t = **7.23**, p = **4.9×10⁻⁵**, 95 % CI **[+0.309, +0.589]**, **10+/0−** — **met as
stated**. Its mean is **24 %** below the n = 3 value (**+0.589 → +0.449**), a fourth instance of §8.4's
seed inflation inside the published 9 %–29 % band.

**The two overstating cells.** The plan fixed before the runs (40 runs, seeds 42–51 × 2 arms × 2 cells;
paired *and* sign-flip tests; `{best, last} × {val, test}`; a prespecified correction) named the strong
branch of its outcome rule: *"both remaining negative and significant with permutation p ≤ 0.002"*.
At n = 10: `p_aitovis` **−1.226 pp** (95 % CI **[−1.370, −1.081]**, t = **−19.17**, p = **1.3×10⁻⁸**,
**0+/10−**, permutation p = **0.001953**) and `p_vistod15` **−3.315 pp**
(**[−3.676, −2.953]**, t = **−20.76**, p = **6.5×10⁻⁹**, **0+/10−**, permutation p = **0.001953** —
2/2¹⁰, the attainable floor at n = 10). Both conditions are met, including **Holm across the two
targets**.

**The raw gap and the marginal cells.** The raw quantity `M_val(ê) − M_test(ê)` ranges from
**−33.0 to +21.2 pp** and cannot be separated into its difficulty and selection parts without per-epoch
test evaluation — which has since been run for **all thirteen** corpora, so the separation is now
available cell by cell (**Table S38**); it does not change the p-values, the confirmatory family or
anything reported in this subsection. Four of 13 cells cross
the uncorrected line; the three marginal ones are `aitod20` **+1.481** (p = **0.054**), `fire`
**+13.658** (p = **0.061**) and `mende20` **+2.472** (p = **0.099**). The **declared** family is this 13-cell scan, and BH at q = 0.05 is applied to it (with **Holm across the two
targets** of the escalation plan); **Table S8 carries the raw p-values only** — the BH-adjusted step is stated
here, not tabulated. The **0 → 3 move after escalation is descriptive**. The three cells BH leaves significant are `p_vistod15` (**6.5×10⁻⁹**), `p_aitovis`
(**1.3×10⁻⁸**) and `shwd2sf` (**0.0053** against a line of **0.0115**), because the two very small
p-values at the front of the ordering lift the lines behind them; `smoke2sf` (**0.0263** against
**0.0154**) does not survive. Before the escalation the smallest p (**0.0053**) lay above the first line
(**0.00385**) and nothing survived.

**The 13-cell family, moved out of the article (effective base: four cells).** The article's
Tier 2 no longer carries the scan itself: it states the effective base (**four** cells at
n = 10, not thirteen) and points here. The sentence displaced from §5.1, verbatim: *"Three
marginal cells **do not cross the line and we make no claim about them**; the 13-cell scan is
descriptive, and **no formal power analysis was run**."* The aggregate row that the article's
Table 2 carried for the remaining cells is likewise here rather than there:
`| other 9 cells | 3–10 | −2.46 to +13.66 | ≥ 0.054 | all include 0 | **not significant** |`
— nine cells that do not cross the uncorrected line, with 95 % intervals including zero.
**Why the base is four.** Nine of the thirteen scanned cells sit at n = 3, and §7.1 of the
article shows that a sign-flip permutation test has **no resolution** there (the smallest
attainable two-sided p is 2/2³ = 0.25). The test is used here as a check at each cell's attainable floor, and it carries the usual assumption: under the null the paired differences are symmetric about zero, i.e. their signs are exchangeable, so those cells are descriptive and carry no inference;
the four cells at n = 10 — the four rows the article's Table 2 retains — are the cells the
direction statement rests on. The cell-by-cell values and the raw and BH-adjusted p-values
side by side are above in this subsection and in Table S8; the 0 → 3 move after the escalation
is descriptive.

**The selection term, cell by cell.** Table 2's caption names the selection contribution proper as the difference `Δprem_val − Δprem_test`; per arm-cell that term is `prem_val − prem_test`, the winner's-curse quantity WC of §3.1 read on the archive. **Verified against the archive, and complete as of 2026-09-30**: the scan record originally carried `prem_test` for **three of the thirteen** corpora, because separating the two sides needs per-epoch test evaluation. That evaluation has now been run — every archived 5-epoch checkpoint of the ten remaining corpora and of the `aitod20` strategy arm, **1,568 checkpoints over 140 runs** (1,848 evaluations including each run's `best`/`last`), on one machine, with the pipeline's registered call and the data YAML read from each run's own `args.yaml` — and the new `test` side **reproduces the archive's own readings**: over the 21 arm-cells carrying both, the val maximum, `prem_val` and the archived `T_best` agree to **max |Δ| = 0.005 pp**, which is the printed rounding; the coverage audit reports 140/140 runs, 1,848 rows, 0 anomalies. `prem_test`, and hence WC, is therefore formable for **all thirteen** corpora and both arms (**Table S38**). Nothing is imputed.

What is recorded for **all thirteen** scanned cells is the val-side selection gain `prem_val = V_max − V_final`, per arm, at the cell's scanned n — baseline → strategy, pp: `shwd2sf` (n = 10) **+1.47 → +0.87**; `smoke2sf` (n = 10) **+0.69 → +0.61**; `p_aitovis` (n = 3) +0.45 → +0.26; `p_vistod15` (n = 3) +1.11 → +0.88; `aitod20` (n = 3) +0.70 → +0.26; `d15d15` (n = 3) +2.13 → +0.67; `dota` (n = 3) +1.20 → +0.67; `fire` (n = 3) +10.95 → +8.87; `mask20` (n = 3) +16.07 → +29.17; `mende20` (n = 3) +4.83 → +5.68; `p_d15toai` (n = 3) +0.49 → +0.39; `p_masktomende` (n = 3) +2.33 → +1.34; `vis` (n = 3) +0.44 → +0.22. Source: `gap_mechanism_20260916.txt`, the same record §5.6 and Table S8 use. The two cells escalated in §5.1 (`p_aitovis`, `p_vistod15`) carry n = 10 after the escalation, which is the four-cell base the article's Table 2 prints.

**The selection term `prem_val − prem_test` where the archive carries it** is the five realization arm-cells of §8.6, read off Table S20's printed values (**the printed `+0.736` / `+0.509` supersede the verbatim S4 dump's third decimal, `0.735` / `0.507`**): `shwd2sf` baseline **+1.469 − 0.736 = +0.733** and strategy **+0.865 − 0.152 = +0.713**; `smoke2sf` baseline **+0.691 − 0.509 = +0.182** and strategy **+0.605 − (−0.157) = +0.762**; `aitod20` baseline (n = 10) **+0.766 − (−0.218) = +0.984**. The other ten scanned corpora and the `aitod20` strategy arm were closed on 2026-09-30 as described immediately above; their cell-by-cell `prem_val`, `prem_test` and WC are in **Table S38**, and read descriptively they carry the same asymmetry — WC positive in 25 of the 26 arm-cells, arm-cell median realization rate 17.2 %, and the realized premium negative in 8 of the 13 strategy arms. These are cell-level readings at each cell's recorded *n*, not an upgrade of any claim. **In arm-difference form** the term is recorded for exactly two cells, from the clean-protocol batch: `shwd2sf` **+0.020** and `smoke2sf` **−0.580**, against Δgap of **+0.779** and **+0.461**. In `smoke2sf` the selection term is therefore **opposite in sign to the Δgap** the article's Tier 2 prints — which is why the direction sentence rests on the four-cell base and **not** on Δgap's sign.

**The sharpest single fact.** In the shwd2sf cell the arms differ on **val** by
60.669 − 60.397 = **+0.272 pp** (baseline better) and on **test** by 43.559 − 44.066 = **−0.507 pp**
(baseline worse): **the sign reverses**, so reading only `val` inverts the arm ordering.

**The endpoint convention, in full.** On `val` the per-epoch maximum; on `test` the checkpoint the
pipeline's own fitness rule kept (`best.pt`), read on the disjoint split. Handing a "selected
checkpoint" to a split therefore always resolves to one of these two endpoints, and no other convention
is used anywhere in this paper. The four paired differences and their intervals are in Table 1; the
Δgap values and readings for the **four printed cells** are in Table 2; the other nine cells of the 13-cell scan are in §5.6 and **Table S10** (seven rows) with the remaining components in `work/gap_mechanism_20260916.txt`.

**Sign-flip permutation tests on the four Tier-1 paired differences.** Table 1 of the article reports a
paired *t* test per cell. Its non-parametric counterpart needs no distributional assumption and is exact
at these *n*: enumerating all 2ⁿ sign flips of the per-seed paired differences
d(seed) = premium_baseline(seed) − premium_strategy(seed), where premium = best-epoch − final-epoch
mAP50-95 on the run's own monitored split, gives

| Cell | n | d̄ | sign split | paired *t* p | exact permutation p | attainable floor 2/2ⁿ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| smoke2sf | 11 | +0.416 | 11+/0− | 1.9×10⁻⁴ | **0.00098** | 0.00098 |
| shwd2sf | 11 | +0.526 | 11+/0− | 7.6×10⁻⁶ | **0.00098** | 0.00098 |
| a2d15 | 10 | +0.272 | 9+/1− | 1.1×10⁻³ | **0.00391** | 0.00195 |
| dota15 | 10 | +0.449 | 10+/0− | 4.9×10⁻⁵ | **0.00195** | 0.00195 |

The permutation test agrees with the paired *t* in every cell. Three of the four sit exactly at the
smallest two-sided *p* attainable at their *n*; `a2d15` carries one negative pair and therefore reaches
4/2¹⁰ = 0.00391, one step above its own floor. Wilcoxon signed-rank returns the same *p* to four decimals
(0.00098 / 0.00098 / 0.00391 / 0.00195). Per-seed values, so that each *p* can be recomputed — the two
*n* = 11 cells carry ten seeded runs plus one unseeded run, listed last:

| Cell | d(seed) |
|---|---|
| smoke2sf | +0.354, +0.247, +0.873, +0.217, +0.738, +0.394, +0.217, +0.088, +0.357, +0.482, +0.611 |
| shwd2sf | +0.305, +0.506, +0.919, +0.466, +0.702, +0.280, +0.515, +0.756, +0.641, +0.354, +0.339 |
| a2d15 | +0.241, +0.211, +0.467, +0.560, +0.324, +0.415, −0.073, +0.216, +0.261, +0.096 |
| dota15 | +0.627, +0.821, +0.524, +0.190, +0.257, +0.369, +0.494, +0.545, +0.226, +0.437 |

`a2d15`'s negative pair is its seventh listed run (seed 48); the other nine are positive. The attainable two-sided
floor for a sign-flip test on *n* pairs is 2/2ⁿ, so the *n* = 11 cells can in principle reach 0.00098
where the *n* = 10 cells cannot go below 0.00195. The `dota15` per-seed values are the ones tabulated in
Table S28; the other three cells' per-seed values are recomputed from the archived per-epoch curves.

**Metric sensitivity: the full mAP50 columns for Tables 1 and 4.** The article reports the headline metric
mAP50-95 throughout and states one metric-sensitivity datum in the text. Both tables recomputed on
`metrics/mAP50(B)`, from the same run sets and the same code path, follow, so that the two metrics can be
compared cell by cell rather than by assertion.

*Table 1 on mAP50 (same cells and runs as the printed table):*

| Cell | n | premium baseline | premium strategy | paired difference | t | p | 95 % CI | per-seed signs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| smoke2sf | 11 | +0.972 | +0.910 | **+0.063** | 0.63 | 0.545 | [−0.160, +0.285] | **6+/5−** |
| shwd2sf | 11 | +1.678 | +0.831 | **+0.847** | 7.00 | 3.7×10⁻⁵ | [+0.577, +1.116] | 11+/0− |
| a2d15 | 10 | +0.803 | +0.396 | **+0.406** | 4.33 | 0.0019 | [+0.194, +0.619] | 9+/1− |
| dota15 | 10 | +1.318 | +0.625 | **+0.693** | 5.67 | 3.0×10⁻⁴ | [+0.417, +0.970] | 9+/1− |

*Table 4 on mAP50 (the same five arm-cells and runs as the printed table):*

| Cell | Arm | n | `prem_val` | `prem_test` | realization rate |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| shwd2sf | baseline | 10 | +1.387 | +1.082 | 78.0 % |
| shwd2sf | strategy | 10 | +0.866 | +0.283 | 32.6 % |
| smoke2sf | baseline | 10 | +0.995 | +0.839 | 84.3 % |
| smoke2sf | strategy | 10 | +0.840 | +0.001 | 0.1 % |
| aitod20 | baseline | 10 | +1.744 | −0.369 | −21.2 % |

Three things the columns show, stated rather than left to the reader. (i) **The sign of the Tier-1 paired
difference is positive in all four cells on both metrics**, and all three cells other than `smoke2sf` keep
significance on mAP50. `smoke2sf` is the single cell that does not: its paired difference is +0.063 pp with
a 95 % interval [−0.160, +0.285] that includes zero, and its per-seed signs are **6+/5−** rather than
unanimous, so on this metric the weakest cell loses both significance and sign unanimity — which is why the
article attributes metric-independence to the *sign* claim and not to any per-seed claim. (ii) On Table 4's
realization rate, mAP50 gives **31.5 %** with a run-level 95 % interval [15.0 %, 48.9 %] — the three
weightings again coincide, every arm-cell being at n = 10 — against 23.3 % on mAP50-95, and the number of
arm-cells with a negative realized premium is **1 of 5** rather than 2 of 5. Partial realization is
therefore directionally metric-robust, while its magnitude and that count are metric-specific. (iii) Both
tables' positive controls are the printed mAP50-95 values, recomputed on the same code path: the four
Tier-1 rows reproduce Table 1 exactly at the printed precision, and the five realization cells reproduce
Table 4 to within 0.001 pp, the aggregate returning 23.26 % against the printed 23.3 %.

**mAP75 is now reported on both sides: the archive still carries no per-epoch mAP75 *reading*, but the per-epoch checkpoints are in register, so the `val` half is re-measured rather than missing — on a five-epoch grid, for four of the five arm-cells.**

**A same-estimand ratio exists elsewhere: the genomics relative-bias literature.** The article's §2.2 scopes its
"no ratio of gains" statement to a ratio of *difference* premiums on disjoint splits in detection; a ratio of the
same estimand — how much of a selected effect survives on an independent split — is however long established in
quantitative genetics, where it is written as a **relative bias** and reported as a number. Würschum & Kraft,
*Cross-validation in association mapping and its relevance for the estimation of QTL parameters of complex traits*,
**Heredity 112(4):463–468 (2014)**, doi 10.1038/hdy.2013.126, define it as `1 − (pG-TS / pG-ES)` — the complement of a
realized-to-selected ratio on a disjoint estimation/test split — and report a mean overestimation of **38 %** for the
proportion of genotypic variance explained by QTL, **30 %** averaged over four further traits, and a literature range
of **10–60 %**; Utz, Melchinger & Schön, *Genetics* 154(4):1839–1849 (2000), had already reported that in independent
validation `p(TS.ES)` "was on average only **50 %** of `p(ES)`". What is not found in that literature, and what this
paper reports, is the ratio of two **difference** premiums — `(T_best − T_final)/(V_max − V_final)` — on disjoint
splits, in detection, on our own five arm-cells, with measured `val` noise as moderator; and the sign flip of
§8.6 (in two of the five arm-cells the realized premium is negative) has no counterpart there either.
**No per-epoch mAP75 reading exists in the archive**: the per-epoch
training logs and every evaluation file in this release record `mAP50` and `mAP50-95` only — a scan of
the 55 metric files behind the two tables above finds no mAP75 column. That is why the **`val` half** of a
mAP75 sensitivity column cannot be *read*; it does **not** block *measuring* it, and it does **not** block
the **test half**. Both halves are analyses of what exists rather than new experiments: `best.pt` and
`last.pt` are the released endpoints for every cell in the two tables, so re-scoring those endpoints at
IoU 0.75 needs no further training; and the per-epoch checkpoints are in register for the four `*2sf`
arm-cells on a **five-epoch grid**, so re-evaluating those checkpoints on `val` at that threshold needs
none either. Both passes have been run and are reported with their scopes below: the test half (82 evaluations, 41 runs, exact positive control against the archived matrix) and the `val` half (880
evaluations, 40 runs, positive control against the training logs at a median absolute difference of
**0.0161 pp**, which is what a re-measurement of a recorded training run should look like). We report the
mAP50 columns (recomputable from the released artifacts) **and** both mAP75 columns — the `val` one on the
five-epoch grid, labelled as such.


### 8.6 The realization rate — measurement detail (moved from the article)

**Scope of the headline number.** The **pooled 55 %** covers the four arm-cells §5.1's escalation adds
(**82 % / 104 %** on `p_aitovis`, **56 % / 29 %** on `p_vistod15`); the two batches are **not pooled**
(different runs, different protocol), so the headline is **not** re-averaged — it is defined over
§8.6's five arm-cells and Table 4 stands. The **104 %** is within noise on ~0.27 pp. Source:
`work/x1_aux_20260918.py`.

**The theory-driven check, in full.** §3.1 derives the realization rate as **1 − WC / premium**, WC being
the winner's-curse part of the selected gain. Two consequences are testable: ① the proposition itself establishes only an expectation, `E[premium_test] = E[premium_val] − WC ≤ E[premium_val]`, and the ratio is defined only where the denominator is positive (`premium_val` > 0); its **per-run shadow is testable rather than proved** — over the **81** runs with both sides measured, WC averages
**+0.463 pp** (t = **8.34**, p = **1.8×10⁻¹²**) and the realized premium is smaller in **68 of 81**
runs; ② the rate should **fall as the `val` curve gets noisier** — sorting those runs by their own
curve's noise scale σ̂ gives terciles of **65.6 % / 49.8 % / 13.8 %**, Spearman **ρ = −0.466**
(p = **1.3×10⁻⁵**, n = **80**). **This section's 23.3 % and §8.7's per-cell variation are therefore the
same statement at different noise levels.** What does **not** hold is the stronger form we first wrote
down — the premium scaling with the noise as σ√(2 ln E): slope **0.026 ± 0.222** (p = **0.82**,
n = **101**) — because the premium's *level* is set by the `val` curve's shape; §12 records the
withdrawal. Scripts: `work/theory_p4_test_20260919.py`, `work/theory_meta_and_tests_20260919.py`. **Re-read with the arm-cell as the resampling unit, as the κ fit of Table S22 already is (81 runs in 9 arm-cells: the 41 three-way runs of §8.6 plus the 40 paired runs of §5.1): WC = +0.463 pp, cluster-robust 95 % CI [+0.204, +0.721], p = 0.0033 (CR1 sandwich on 9 clusters; a cluster bootstrap gives [+0.253, +0.661]); the noise-rate Spearman ρ = −0.466, cluster-bootstrap 95 % CI [−0.651, −0.131], p = 0.0115 — and at the 9-cluster level ρ = −0.433, p = 0.244 (work/r102_cluster_20260927.py).**

**The three weightings.** The three figures are ratios of means computed three ways — runs weighted
equally, unweighted arm-cell means, and arm-cells with n ≥ 5 — and because every cell carries n = 10
they coincide at **23.3 %**. On the previous **41**-run sample they did not, under the same declared
`prem_val = V_max − V_final` endpoint as Table 4: the arm-cell-equal reading was **19.6 %**, the
run-weighted one **32.4 %**, and the arm-cells-with-n ≥ 5 one **34.5 %** — a ~15 pp spread
coming from a single cell holding **1** run. Each arm-cell is at n = 10 here, so the estimator's own
interval is reported instead: 95 % CI **[8.4 %, 37.4 %]**, run-level bootstrap,
P(rate ≤ 0) = **0.002**.

**The two intervals, and the primary one.** The run-level bootstrap on the same 50 runs gives 95 % CI **[8.4 %, 37.4 %]** with P(rate ≤ 0) = **0.002**; every `P(rate ≤ 0)` in this subsection is a **bootstrap tail proportion** (B = 20,000 resamples, seed 20260927), not a frequentist p-value, and the only multiplicity correction this paper applies is the **Benjamini–Hochberg step over the declared 13-cell family** (Holm, where it appears, belongs to the **two escalation targets**); p-values printed elsewhere, including the paired t values, are **raw**, the **arm-cell-equal (five-cluster) bootstrap** gives **[−17.3 %, 53.2 %]** with P(rate ≤ 0) = **0.118**, and it is the interval the claim is read at, because the claim is about five arm-cells. The five-cell rates are 50 %, 18 %, 74 %, −26 % and −28 % (Table S20), i.e. a median of **17.6 %**, a span of **−28.5 % to +73.7 %** (**102.2** pp from the printed endpoints, 102.1 pp from the unrounded cells) and a leave-one-out range of **9.77 % to 34.16 %** — dropping the `shwd2sf` baseline arm-cell gives 9.77 %, dropping the `aitod20` baseline arm-cell gives 34.16 %. Script `work/item2_final_v2_20260925.py`; the two-cell negative set is Table 4's **2/5**.

**§5.1's escalation in this section's units.** The four arm-cells it adds are **82 % / 104 %**
(`p_aitovis`) and **56 % / 29 %** (`p_vistod15`), pooled **55 %** against **23.3 %**.

**Metric and endpoint detail.** `best.pt × val` versus the per-epoch maximum in `results.csv` differs by
up to **0.083 pp**: the selection action and the readout action can differ; the earlier attribution to a `best.pt` selection rule other than mAP50-95 is **withdrawn** — in the pinned v8.4.120 `fitness ≡ mAP50-95` on the monitored split — and the residual is recorded as **unidentified**. `last.pt`'s val recomputation differs from the final `results.csv` row
by **~0.02 pp**.

**The closest concurrent work, moved out of the article.** The article's §8.6 no longer carries this comparison; it is recorded here. **[15]** splits selection leakage into diversity and noise over **2,047** tabular classification datasets (the noise share is reported, the non-tabular case is undone). **[12]** measures checkpoint selection raising a reported score by **6.24 pp** on EEG when the candidate set grows from **5 to 80** — and, in the same table, **−1.24 pp** (95 % interval **−2.67 to 0.26**) on its other pool, so **its own sign does not reproduce across its two pools**. This paper instead uses **our own five arm-cells**, reports a **ratio**, on **detection**, with **measured `val` noise** as moderator (the third-party logs of §5.5 corroborate the same component, but they carry no `test` side and so cannot form this ratio). **No number left the paper when this paragraph moved**: every value above is the article's former wording.

### 8.6b The eleven arm-cells with both sides measured: a batch-stratified re-aggregation, no new training

> **What this is, and what it is not.** The article's realization rate rests on **five** arm-cells, and a
> reviewer asked for every arm-cell whose **two sides** are both in the archive, aggregated with the
> **batch as the stratum**. This section is that aggregation, and it is a **re-reading of measurements this
> submission already reports** — the per-epoch `val` curves and the `best`/`last` × `test` readouts of runs
> that already exist. **It contains no new training, no new seed, no new evaluation and no new data.**
> Nothing in the article changes because of it: the five-cell rate of §8.6, its interval and its scope
> stand, and this is a wider reading of the same archive, not a replacement headline.

**The eleven, by batch.** A cluster is one `corpus` × `arm` pair measured on **both** sides, paired inside
the run: `prem_val = max_e V(e) − V(e_f)` from the run's own `results.csv`, and `prem_test = T_best − T_final`
from its `test` readouts; a cluster's rate is `prem_test / prem_val`, and a pooled rate is the ratio of the
summed means.

* **Batch 1 — the article's headline batch** (Table S20 / Table 4; three corpus pairs, n = 10 per cluster,
  50 runs; **5 clusters**). Cluster rates: `shwd2sf` baseline **50.1 %**, `shwd2sf` strategy **17.6 %**,
  `smoke2sf` baseline **73.7 %**, `smoke2sf` strategy **−26.0 %**, `aitod20` baseline **−28.4 %**; median
  **17.6 %**, span **−28.4 % to +73.7 %** (**102.1** pp from these cells; **102.2** pp from the printed endpoints) — the article prints the lower end as −28.5 %,
  from Table 4's 3-decimal cells, the full-precision value being **−28.39 %** — **2/5** negative,
  run-weighted **23.26 %**, cell-level 95 % CI **[−17.3 %, 53.2 %]** (P(rate ≤ 0) = **0.118**). This batch
  *is* §8.6.
* **Batch 2 — both arms of the two Tier-2 cells §5.1 escalated** (§5.1; n = 10 per cluster, 40 runs;
  **4 clusters**). `p_aitovis` baseline **81.7 %**, `p_aitovis` strategy **103.7 %**, `p_vistod15`
  baseline **55.7 %**, `p_vistod15` strategy **28.8 %** — the four values the article prints as
  **82 % / 104 %** and **56 % / 29 %**, pooled **55 %**; median **68.7 %**, span **+28.8 % to +103.7 %**,
  **0/4** negative, run-weighted **55.0 %**.
* **Batch 3 — the third-corpus replication** (§3.2, G4; `mende20`, two arms × ten seeds, 20 runs;
  **2 clusters**). Baseline **41.5 %**, strategy **33.8 %**; median **37.6 %**, span **+33.8 % to
  +41.5 %**, **0/2** negative, run-weighted **37.5 %**. Its winner's-curse term is the **+5.212 pp** that
  Table S33 already prints.
* **All eleven, batch as the stratum.** Median **41.5 %**, mean **39.3 %**, span **−28.4 % to +103.7 %**
  (**132.1** pp), **2/11** negative; run-weighted pooled **36.9 %** over **110** runs. A cluster bootstrap
  that resamples arm-cells **within** their batch (B = 20,000; seed 20260927) gives 95 % CI
  **[19.3 %, 60.4 %]** for the arm-cell-equal statistic, **[28.9 %, 44.4 %]** for the run-weighted one, and
  **[26.2 %, 60.8 %]** for the median — each with P(rate ≤ 0) = **0.000**.
* **Difference scale, the reviewer's other request** (`prem_test − prem_val`, pp, clusters equally
  weighted): **−1.367 pp**, 95 % CI **[−1.59, −1.15]**. It is reported with a warning rather than as a
  co-primary, because a pp difference is not scale-free: the mean is carried by the batch with the largest
  `prem_val` — batch 3 is **−5.212 pp**, against **−0.675 pp** (batch 1) and **−0.310 pp** (batch 2).
* **The other axes.** By **arm**: six baseline-style (`base100`) clusters, median **52.9 %**, one negative,
  against five learning-rate-style (`lr005_100ep`) clusters, median **28.8 %**, one negative. By
  **budget**: ten clusters ran **E = 100** and one (`aitod20` baseline) ran **E = 30**, too lopsided to be
  read as a budget effect. By **corpus family**: four clusters transfer into **SFCHD** (`shwd2sf`,
  `smoke2sf`), four come from the `p_aitovis` / `p_vistod15` pair, one is **AI-TOD** on its 20 % subset,
  and two are `mende20`. By **metric**: the five-cluster `mAP50` column is the one printed in the **5.1**
  measurement detail of this supplement (**31.5 %**, **1/5** negative, against 23.3 % and 2/5 on
  mAP50-95); **this section does not extend that column to the other two batches**, and no eleven-cluster
  mAP50 rate is stated.

**The five arm-cells' denominators, moved from the article.** The article says the difference-scale reading rests on a denominator that spans **+0.605 to +1.469 pp** across the five arm-cells; the per-cell values are in §S4's verbatim dump above (`smoke2sf` strategy **+0.605**, `shwd2sf` baseline **+1.469**, and the three further cells in Table S20).

**What the wider reading does not license.** At eleven clusters the arm-cell-equal interval no longer
includes zero, where at five it did; that is the outcome and it is stated rather than smoothed. It does
**not** upgrade the article's claim. ① The six added clusters **were not run for this question**: the two
Tier-2 cells were escalated *because* their `val` overstatement had already been flagged, and the third
corpus replicates the same protocol, so the eleven are not an exchangeable sample of corpora. ② The strata
differ in **level** — batch 1 spans **−28.4 % to +73.7 %**, batch 2 **+28.8 % to +103.7 %** — which is why
the batch is the stratum and no single pooled rate is printed as *the* rate. ③ It is still a **ratio of
small means**: `prem_val` across the eleven clusters runs from **+0.268 pp** to **+8.723 pp**, so a few
hundredths of a point in a numerator move a cluster's rate by tens of points. ④ The reviewer's own power
note applies: at this between-cluster spread, clearing zero at cluster level would take of the order of
**25** clusters, and the interval above excludes zero **for these eleven archived clusters**, which is all
it says.

**What is paired, what is single-sided, and what has no denominator.**

* **Paired on both sides, on the same run: all eleven clusters**, and within a batch the two arms share
  their seeds. No cluster in the eleven is assembled from different runs on its two sides.
* **Single-sided or off-metric, and therefore outside the eleven.** ① The epoch-budget series (§3.2, G3; Tables S32 and
  S34b) carries **`prem_val` only**: its readout side is the disjoint carve **C3** (100 images), not the
  published `test` split, and the archive holds no per-epoch `test` curve for it, so no `prem_test` — and
  hence no rate in this section's sense — can be formed; its own readout is recorded where it belongs
  (WC = `prem_val` − `prem_C3` is negative in all **16** readouts, mean **−0.616 pp**, Table S32).
  ② The IoU-0.75 pass now carries **both sides** for the four `*2sf` arm-cells: the `val` half is
  re-measured from the in-register checkpoints (880 evaluations, five-epoch grid), so the one cluster it
